PR-41  Comparison and alternatives mining
 KEYWORDS · INTERMEDIATE
When to use
When competitors are named in your sales calls or AI answers.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to produce misleading competitor claims — outputs feed honest comparisons only.
Required inputs
Competitor names, honest differentiators, known areas where they are stronger.
Databases used
Keyword Master, Content, AI Competitors
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are mining comparison and alternative queries.
OBJECTIVE: Identify the vs and alternative queries worth covering honestly.
CONTEXT: Competitors: [LIST]. Our honest differentiators: [PASTE]. Where they are
genuinely stronger: [PASTE]. Existing comparison pages: [LIST].
CONSTRAINTS: Every comparison page must be able to concede something true. Do not
generate queries we could only answer by misrepresenting a competitor. Do not
make factual claims about competitor products.
PROCESS: 1 Generate vs, alternative, and switching queries per competitor.
2 Assess whether we can answer each honestly. 3 Identify which need a concession
to be credible. 4 Flag any query we should not target.
OUTPUT: A comparison query table with an honesty assessment, the required
concession per page, and the queries to decline.
QUALITY CHECKS: Every targetable query can be answered honestly; concessions are
identified.
MISSING INFORMATION: If we do not know where competitors are stronger, say the
comparison would not be credible.
HUMAN REVIEW: Marketing approves every comparison claim before publication.
Expected output
A comparison query table with honesty assessments and required concessions.
Quality checklist
Every page can concede something true; no competitor product claims.
Failure conditions
Declines when competitor strengths are unknown.

PR-42  Conversational query generation
 KEYWORDS · INTERMEDIATE
When to use
When building the prompt universe or covering assistant-style questions.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to generate keyword-shaped strings — these must read as speech.
Required inputs
Question bank, buyer decisions, constraints buyers mention.
Databases used
Search Demand, AI Prompt Universe, Question Bank
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are generating conversational queries as people ask assistants.
OBJECTIVE: Produce natural-language questions for coverage and prompt tracking.
CONTEXT: Question bank: [PASTE]. Buyer decisions: [LIST]. Constraints buyers
mention: [PASTE, e.g. team size, budget, existing stack].
CONSTRAINTS: Phrase as a person speaks, including the constraint. No keyword
strings. Each question must be answerable factually rather than promotionally.
PROCESS: 1 For each decision, write how someone would ask about it aloud.
2 Add constraint variations. 3 Add comparison and recommendation phrasings.
4 Mark which are suitable for the tracked prompt universe. 5 Note what a good
answer contains.
OUTPUT: A conversational query set with constraint variants, prompt-universe
candidates flagged, and good-answer definitions.
QUALITY CHECKS: Phrasing is speech-like; constraints included; good answers
defined.
MISSING INFORMATION: Without a question bank, mark output as inferred and
lower quality.
HUMAN REVIEW: Wording is fixed before any prompt enters the tracked set.
Expected output
A conversational query set with constraints and prompt-universe candidates.
Quality checklist
Speech-like phrasing; constraints present; good answers defined.
Failure conditions
Marks output inferred without a question bank.

PR-43
PR-43  Keyword-to-page mapping
 KEYWORDS · FOUNDATIONAL
When to use
After scoring, before anything is commissioned.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to map several similar keywords to several similar pages.
Required inputs
Scored keyword list, existing page inventory with topics.
Databases used
Keyword Master, Content, Topic Clusters
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are mapping scored keywords to owning pages.
OBJECTIVE: Assign exactly one owning page per keyword, or flag a gap.
CONTEXT: Scored keywords: [PASTE with intent and stage]. Existing pages: [PASTE
URLs and topics]. Cluster plan: [PASTE].
CONSTRAINTS: One keyword, one owning page. A page may support several related
variants but owns exactly one primary. Where two keywords want the same page,
one must be reassigned or declined.
PROCESS: 1 Match each keyword to the page whose intent it fits. 2 Flag keywords
with no suitable page as gaps. 3 Flag pages assigned more than one primary.
4 Resolve conflicts by intent, not by preference. 5 Recommend merge or
differentiate where pages overlap.
OUTPUT: A mapping table, the gap list, the conflict list with resolutions, and
merge recommendations.
QUALITY CHECKS: No page holds two primaries; conflicts resolved by intent.
MISSING INFORMATION: If page topics were not supplied, mapping is marked
provisional.
HUMAN REVIEW: The content lead confirms before commissioning.
Expected output
A mapping table with gaps, conflicts, and merge recommendations.
Quality checklist
One primary per page; conflicts resolved on intent.
Failure conditions
Marks mapping provisional without page topic data.

PR-44  Cannibalization diagnosis
 KEYWORDS · ADVANCED
When to use
When ranking URLs for a query change back and forth over weeks.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to justify deleting a page before checking its links and traffic.
Required inputs
Query-level ranking URL history over 90 days, page inventory, internal links.
Databases used
Keyword Master, Content, Internal Link Opportunities
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are diagnosing keyword cannibalization.
OBJECTIVE: Identify conflicts and recommend one resolution each.
CONTEXT: Ranking URL history by query, 90 days: [PASTE]. Page inventory: [PASTE].
Internal links to each competing page: [PASTE].
CONSTRAINTS: A conflict means two of our URLs alternate for one query, not merely
that two pages mention it. Recommend one resolution per conflict: consolidate,
differentiate, de-optimize, or redirect. Never recommend deletion without
checking links and traffic.
PROCESS: 1 Identify queries with alternating URLs. 2 Determine which page should
own each. 3 Check internal links — they often point at the wrong page. 4 Choose a
resolution. 5 Specify the internal link changes required.
OUTPUT: A conflict table with the intended owner, the resolution, the internal
link changes, and a 60-day recheck date.
QUALITY CHECKS: Alternation evidenced; one resolution each; link changes
specified.
MISSING INFORMATION: Without ranking history, say the diagnosis is speculative.
HUMAN REVIEW: The SEO lead approves any redirect or removal.
Expected output
A conflict table with resolutions, link changes, and a recheck date.
Quality checklist
Alternation evidenced; one resolution per conflict; links addressed.
Failure conditions
Declares the diagnosis speculative without ranking history.

PR-45
PR-45  Ranking tracking rules
 KEYWORDS · FOUNDATIONAL
When to use
Once at setup, and when the tracked set grows unmanageable.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to justify tracking everything — the discipline is what to exclude.
Required inputs
Keyword list with scores, business priorities, tool limits.
Databases used
Keyword Master, Reports
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are defining what gets rank-tracked and how often.
OBJECTIVE: Produce a tracking set small enough to review and large enough to
matter.
CONTEXT: Keyword list with priority scores: [PASTE]. Business priorities:
[DESCRIBE]. Tool capacity: [DESCRIBE]. Markets and devices: [LIST].
CONSTRAINTS: Track what a decision depends on. Exclude anything you would not act
on. Define frequency by volatility, not by habit. State the review cadence.
PROCESS: 1 Select the tracked set by score and business proximity. 2 Assign
frequency per group. 3 Define the market and device split. 4 State what is
deliberately untracked and why. 5 Define who reviews and when.
OUTPUT: A tracking specification with the set, frequency, splits, exclusions, and
the review cadence.
QUALITY CHECKS: Exclusions are explicit; frequency justified by volatility.
MISSING INFORMATION: Without tool limits, produce the ideal set and note it may
need trimming.
HUMAN REVIEW: The SEO lead approves the exclusion list.
Expected output
A tracking specification with an explicit exclusion list and cadence.
Quality checklist
Exclusions stated; frequency justified; a reviewer named.
Failure conditions
Produces an ideal set and flags trimming when limits are unknown.

Quality checklist
Every spoke evidenced; interlinking specified; commercial path present.
Failure conditions

PR-49  Original research design
 CONTENT PLANNING · ADVANCED
When to use
When you have first-party data and want a genuinely citable asset.
When NOT to use
Do not use it where the data cannot support a finding — a weak study damages credibility.
Required inputs
The available data, its provenance, permissions, and known limitations.
Databases used
Content, Digital PR Campaigns
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are designing an original research asset from first-party data.
OBJECTIVE: Determine what the data can honestly support, and design around it.
CONTEXT: Data available: [DESCRIBE, including how it was collected]. Sample size:
[N]. Permissions: [WHAT MAY BE PUBLISHED]. Known limitations: [DESCRIBE].
CONSTRAINTS: Never propose a finding the data cannot support. State sample size
and methodology alongside every finding. Do not extrapolate to a population the
sample does not represent. Anonymize completely.
PROCESS: 1 State what the data can and cannot support. 2 Identify the findings
that are genuinely novel. 3 Design the methodology statement. 4 Draft the
limitations section before the findings. 5 Identify the strongest citable claim.
OUTPUT: A research design with supportable findings, the methodology statement,
the limitations section, and the citable claim.
QUALITY CHECKS: Limitations written before findings; no extrapolation beyond the
sample; anonymization confirmed.
MISSING INFORMATION: If the sample cannot support a finding, say so and
recommend not publishing.
HUMAN REVIEW: The data owner and legal confirm what may be published.
Expected output
A research design with supportable findings, methodology, and limitations.
Quality checklist
Limitations precede findings; no extrapolation; anonymization confirmed.
Failure conditions
Recommends not publishing when the sample cannot support a finding.

PR-55  Content measurement loop
 CONTENT PLANNING · INTERMEDIATE
When to use
Sixty and ninety days after a piece publishes.
When NOT to use
Do not use it before sixty days — you will be reading noise.
Required inputs
The piece, its brief, its target keyword, and performance since publication.
Databases used
Content, Keyword Master, Reports
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are reviewing whether a published piece did what the brief intended.
OBJECTIVE: Produce a verdict and one next action.
CONTEXT: The piece: [URL and summary]. Brief intent: [PASTE]. Target keyword:
[PASTE]. Performance since publication: [PASTE with dates]. Internal links
added: [LIST].
CONSTRAINTS: Compare against the brief's stated intent, not against a general
expectation. Sixty days minimum. Distinguish a ranking problem from a conversion
problem from a demand problem.
PROCESS: 1 State what the brief intended. 2 Compare against actual performance.
3 Classify any shortfall by type. 4 Check whether the planned internal links were
actually added. 5 Recommend one action.
OUTPUT: A verdict against brief intent, the shortfall classification, the link
verification, and one next action with an owner.
QUALITY CHECKS: Measured against the brief; shortfall type classified; links
verified.
MISSING INFORMATION: Under sixty days, decline to judge and set a review date.
HUMAN REVIEW: The content lead decides the next action.
Expected output
A verdict against brief intent with a classified shortfall and one action.
Quality checklist
Brief-relative judgement; shortfall classified; links verified.
Failure conditions
Declines to judge before sixty days.

PR-59  Direct-answer block writing
 ON-PAGE · FOUNDATIONAL
When to use
On any page targeting a question, before publication.
When NOT to use

Required inputs

Databases used
Content, Question Bank
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are writing a direct-answer block for extractability.
OBJECTIVE: Produce a passage that answers the question accurately when quoted
alone.
CONTEXT: The question: [QUESTION]. The correct answer: [PASTE]. Caveats: [PASTE].
Sources for any figure: [PASTE].
CONSTRAINTS: Under 60 words. Must remain accurate with no surrounding context.
Every figure carries its unit, period, and source. No hedging that removes the
answer. No promotional language.
PROCESS: 1 State the answer in one sentence. 2 Add the essential qualification.
3 Attach figures with units and sources. 4 Test the passage in isolation.
5 Rewrite anything that depends on prior context.
OUTPUT: The answer block, the isolation test result, and any caveat that could
not be included without breaking the word limit.
QUALITY CHECKS: Accurate in isolation; under 60 words; figures fully attributed.
MISSING INFORMATION: If the answer requires a caveat that will not fit, say so
rather than dropping it.
HUMAN REVIEW: The subject expert confirms accuracy.
Expected output
A sub-60-word answer block with an isolation test result.
Quality checklist
Accurate alone; figures attributed; no dropped caveats.
Failure conditions
Refuses to drop a caveat to meet the word limit.

CONSTRAINTS: Only questions people actually asked. Answers under 50 words, direct,
and accurate in isolation. Do not include a question to justify markup. Every
answer must be visible on the page, not only in the markup.
PROCESS: 1 Select questions by frequency and page relevance. 2 Write each answer
directly, without preamble. 3 Test each answer in isolation. 4 Confirm each is
visible on the page. 5 Note which need a source.
OUTPUT: The FAQ block with sources noted, the isolation test results, and any
question excluded with the reason.
QUALITY CHECKS: Real questions only; answers visible on the page; accurate alone.
MISSING INFORMATION: Without a question bank, produce nothing and say why.
HUMAN REVIEW: The editor confirms every answer is visible before markup is added.
Expected output
An FAQ block from real questions with isolation test results.
Quality checklist
Real questions only; visible answers; accurate in isolation.
Failure conditions
Produces nothing without a real question source.

PR-64  Crawl budget analysis
 TECHNICAL SEO · ADVANCED
When to use
When index coverage or crawl frequency suggests waste.
When NOT to use
Do not use it without log data — everything else is inference.
Required inputs
Server logs filtered by crawler, plus the URL inventory.
Databases used
Technical Issues, Site Architecture
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are analysing crawl budget distribution from log data.
OBJECTIVE: Find where crawl effort goes versus where it should.
CONTEXT: Log sample: [PASTE, filtered by user agent, with the period]. URL
inventory with commercial importance: [PASTE]. Known parameter patterns: [LIST].
CONSTRAINTS: Log data only — do not infer crawl behaviour from index coverage.
State the sample period. Do not recommend blocking anything without checking
whether it is indexed and ranking.
PROCESS: 1 Group requests by URL pattern. 2 Compare share of crawl against
commercial importance. 3 Identify patterns consuming disproportionate budget.
4 Check whether those URLs are indexed or ranking. 5 Recommend action per
pattern.
OUTPUT: A crawl distribution table, the disproportionate patterns, their index
status, and a recommended action each.
QUALITY CHECKS: Log-based only; sample period stated; index status checked before
any blocking recommendation.
MISSING INFORMATION: Without logs, state that the analysis cannot be performed.
HUMAN REVIEW: Engineering confirms before any robots or parameter change.
Expected output
A crawl distribution table with actions per disproportionate pattern.
Quality checklist
Log-based; period stated; index status checked before blocking.
Failure conditions
Declines the analysis entirely without log data.

PR-66  Migration redirect mapping
 TECHNICAL SEO · ADVANCED
When to use
Before any URL structure change, however small.
When NOT to use
Do not use it after launch — a redirect map built retrospectively always loses the tail.
Required inputs
Full current URL inventory with traffic, rankings, and backlinks; the new structure.
Databases used
Technical Issues, Content, Backlinks
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are building a migration redirect map.
OBJECTIVE: Ensure every current URL has a destination, one hop, before launch.
CONTEXT: Current URL inventory: [PASTE with traffic, rankings, backlinks]. New
structure: [DESCRIBE]. URLs being removed entirely: [LIST].
CONSTRAINTS: Every URL gets a destination — no exceptions, including the long
tail. One hop only. Redirect to the closest equivalent, never blanket to the
homepage. Flag any URL with backlinks being removed.
PROCESS: 1 Map every URL to its new destination. 2 Identify URLs with no
equivalent and choose the closest relevant page. 3 Flag chains. 4 Flag
backlinked URLs being removed. 5 Produce the verification list for post-launch.
OUTPUT: The complete redirect map, the no-equivalent decisions with reasoning,
the chain flags, and the post-launch verification list.
QUALITY CHECKS: Every URL mapped; one hop; no blanket homepage redirects;
backlinked removals flagged.
MISSING INFORMATION: If the inventory is partial, state the map is incomplete and
name what is missing.
HUMAN REVIEW: Engineering and SEO both sign the map before launch.
Expected output
A complete one-hop redirect map with verification list.
Quality checklist
Every URL mapped; no blanket redirects; backlinked removals flagged.
Failure conditions
Declares the map incomplete on a partial inventory.

PR-67  Incident response triage
 TECHNICAL SEO · ADVANCED
When to use
The moment a sitewide traffic or indexing anomaly appears.
When NOT to use
Do not use it for gradual declines — those are decay, not incidents.
Required inputs
What changed, when it started, recent releases, and current status codes.
Databases used
Technical Issues, Technical Releases & Verification
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are triaging a suspected SEO incident.
OBJECTIVE: Determine what changed, when, and what to check first.
CONTEXT: What is observed: [DESCRIBE with dates]. Recent releases: [PASTE the
release log]. Current robots.txt: [PASTE]. Current index coverage: [PASTE].
Status codes on key pages: [PASTE].
CONSTRAINTS: Check the release log before anything else — most incidents are a
deploy. Do not speculate about algorithm updates until site-side causes are
eliminated. Order checks by how quickly they can be ruled out.
PROCESS: 1 Correlate the start date against the release log. 2 Check robots and
meta directives. 3 Check status codes and canonicals on affected templates.
4 Check index coverage change. 5 Only then consider external causes.
OUTPUT: A ranked check list with what each rules in or out, the most likely
cause, and the immediate action.
QUALITY CHECKS: Release log checked first; site-side causes eliminated before
external ones; checks ordered by speed.
MISSING INFORMATION: Without a release log, say that diagnosis will be
substantially slower and start one.
HUMAN REVIEW: Engineering is engaged immediately on any critical finding.
Expected output
A ranked diagnostic checklist with the most likely cause and an action.
Quality checklist
Release log first; site-side before external; speed-ordered checks.
Failure conditions
Flags the absence of a release log as a diagnostic handicap.

PR-68  Internal link opportunity generation
 INTERNAL LINKING · FOUNDATIONAL
When to use
Monthly, and after any batch of publishing.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to link every page to every other — relevance below 3 is discarded.
Required inputs
The source page, candidate destinations with commercial importance, existing links.
Databases used
Internal Link Opportunities, Content
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are generating internal link opportunities from one page.
OBJECTIVE: Suggest links a reader would genuinely want, prioritizing commercial
destinations.
CONTEXT: Source page: [PASTE full text]. Candidate destinations: [LIST with
topics and commercial importance]. Existing links on this page: [LIST].
CONSTRAINTS: Maximum six suggestions. Each needs a reader-benefit rationale.
Relevance below 3 is discarded rather than included. Anchors must vary. Do not
suggest a link purely because the destination is commercially important.
PROCESS: 1 Identify natural link moments in the text. 2 Match each to the most
relevant destination. 3 Write a descriptive anchor. 4 Score target value,
relevance, source strength. 5 Discard anything below 3 on relevance.
OUTPUT: A table with the sentence to link from, the anchor, the destination, the
three scores, the priority product, and the reader rationale.
QUALITY CHECKS: Every link has a rationale; anchors vary; low relevance
discarded.
MISSING INFORMATION: Without commercial importance, rank by relevance and say so.
HUMAN REVIEW: The editor approves before implementation.
Expected output
A scored link table with anchors, destinations, and reader rationales.
Quality checklist
Rationale per link; varied anchors; low-relevance discarded.
Failure conditions
Ranks on relevance alone when importance is unknown.

PR-69  Orphan page resolution

CONSTRAINTS: An orphan with no traffic, no links, and no cluster fit is a removal

PROCESS: 1 Classify each orphan: valuable, marginal, or obsolete. 2 For valuable
ones, identify relevant source pages. 3 For marginal ones, assess merge

destination, and the backlink flags.

relevant sources; obsolete pages are not linked for the sake of it.

Expected output
A per-orphan decision table with actions, sources, and backlink flags.
Quality checklist
Backlinks checked; relevance required; removal is a valid outcome.
Failure conditions
Defers removals without backlink data.

PR-75  Toxic link review
 AUTHORITY AND PR · ADVANCED

COPY-PASTE PROMPT

inherited, or organic. 3 Check for a manual action or a clear ranking event.
4 Recommend removal attempts first. 5 Only then consider disavowal, with the
reasoning recorded.

disavowal recommendation with its reasoning — or an explicit recommendation to
take no action.
QUALITY CHECKS: Pattern evidenced; origin determined; removal attempted first;
no action is a valid outcome.
MISSING INFORMATION: Without evidence of a problem, recommend no action.
HUMAN REVIEW: The SEO lead approves any disavowal, and the reasoning is logged.
Expected output
A pattern assessment with a removal plan and a documented recommendation.
Quality checklist
Pattern required; removal first; no action is valid.
Failure conditions
Recommends no action absent evidence of an actual problem.

OBJECTIVE: Record mention, citation, sentiment, errors, and sources consistently.
CONTEXT: Prompt exactly as run: [PASTE]. Assistant: [NAME]. Date: [DATE]. Answer
verbatim: [PASTE]. Our brand fact sheet: [PASTE]. Competitor list: [LIST].
CONSTRAINTS: Mention and citation are separate — record both. Compare every
statement about us against the fact sheet. Do not speculate about why the system
answered this way. Quote errors exactly.
PROCESS: 1 Record whether we were mentioned. 2 Record whether we were cited with
attribution. 3 Note position in the answer if named. 4 Classify sentiment.
5 Check every claim about us against the fact sheet. 6 List competitors named and
sources cited.
OUTPUT: A classification record with all fields, the factual errors quoted, and
the source list.
QUALITY CHECKS: Mention and citation separate; errors quoted exactly; no
speculation about system behaviour.
MISSING INFORMATION: If the prompt wording differs from the universe record, void
the run.
HUMAN REVIEW: Factual errors escalate to the brand facts owner immediately.
Expected output
A full classification record with quoted errors and a source list.
Quality checklist
Mention and citation separated; errors quoted; no speculation.
Failure conditions
Voids the run if prompt wording drifted.

PR-80  Share of AI voice calculation
 LLM AND AI SEO · INTERMEDIATE
When to use
Monthly, once every core prompt has been run.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to compare against another company or a published figure.
Required inputs
All runs for the month with mentions and competitors named.
Databases used
AI Prompt Runs, Historical Scorecards, AI Competitors
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are calculating the monthly AI visibility measures.
OBJECTIVE: Produce five comparable measures with their limitations stated.
CONTEXT: All runs this month: [PASTE]. Prompt set size: [N]. Assistants: [LIST].
Previous month's scorecard: [PASTE].
CONSTRAINTS: State the prompt set size and assistant list with every figure. If
the set changed, flag the month as not comparable. Never present share of voice
as market share. Report method alongside every number.
PROCESS: 1 Calculate mention rate, citation rate, answer coverage, share of AI
voice, accuracy rate. 2 Compare with the previous month. 3 Check whether the set
changed. 4 State the limitation of each measure. 5 Note hypotheses for movement,
labelled as hypotheses.
OUTPUT: The five measures with method, the month-on-month comparison, the
comparability flag, and hypotheses clearly labelled.
QUALITY CHECKS: Set size stated; comparability flagged; hypotheses labelled as
such; no market-share framing.
MISSING INFORMATION: If any core prompt was not run, the scorecard is provisional.
HUMAN REVIEW: The SEO lead approves before it enters leadership reporting.
Expected output
Five measures with method, comparison, and labelled hypotheses.
Quality checklist
Set size stated; comparability flagged; no market-share claims.
Failure conditions
Marks the scorecard provisional if a core prompt was skipped.

PR-81
PR-81  Citation gap prioritization
 LLM AND AI SEO · ADVANCED
When to use
Monthly, after the tracking run, to convert gaps into work.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to target sources with no realistic route to inclusion.
Required inputs
Sources cited across runs, whether they mention us, and our available assets.
Databases used
AI Citations & Sources, Authority Targets, Content
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are prioritizing citation gaps into actionable work.
OBJECTIVE: Rank the sources worth pursuing and specify a route for each.
CONTEXT: Sources cited this month: [PASTE with frequency]. Which mention us:
[PASTE]. Which mention competitors: [PASTE]. Our assets: [LIST]. Our capacity:
[DESCRIBE].
CONSTRAINTS: Rank by citation frequency and closability, not by domain metric.
Every entry needs a specific route. Exclude sources with no legitimate route.
Never propose payment or concealment.
PROCESS: 1 Identify sources covering competitors and not us. 2 Rank by how often
they appear across the prompt set. 3 Assess closability. 4 Specify a route per
closable gap. 5 Estimate effort and sequence.
OUTPUT: A ranked gap table with frequency, closability, route, effort, and the
excluded sources with reasons.
QUALITY CHECKS: Ranked on frequency and closability; every entry has a route; no
payment or concealment.
MISSING INFORMATION: Without frequency data, rank on relevance and say so.
HUMAN REVIEW: The outreach owner takes the closable gaps into the target list.
Expected output
A ranked citation gap table with routes, effort, and exclusions.
Quality checklist
Frequency and closability ranking; routes specified; nothing paid or hidden.
Failure conditions
Ranks on relevance alone without frequency data.

PR-82  Factual error correction plan
 LLM AND AI SEO · ADVANCED
When to use
Immediately, whenever a tracked answer states something incorrect about you.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to attempt to influence a model directly — corrections happen at source.
Required inputs
The error, the correct fact, and the sources that may be causing it.
Databases used
Brand Facts, AI Prompt Runs, Local Citations, Content
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are planning a correction for a factual error in an AI answer.
OBJECTIVE: Find the likely source of the error and correct it there.
CONTEXT: The error, quoted: [PASTE]. The correct fact from our sheet: [PASTE].
Where this fact appears publicly: [PASTE all sources]. Assistant and date:
[PASTE].
CONSTRAINTS: Corrections happen at source. Do not propose prompt-based attempts
to influence a model. Identify every public source stating the wrong version.
Correct owned properties first.
PROCESS: 1 Search our own properties for the incorrect version. 2 Search third-
party sources for it. 3 Rank by likely influence. 4 Correct owned sources
immediately. 5 Draft correction requests for third parties. 6 Set a recheck date.
OUTPUT: A source list ranked by likely influence, the owned-source corrections,
drafted third-party requests, and the recheck date.
QUALITY CHECKS: Owned sources corrected first; no attempt to influence the model
directly; recheck date set.
MISSING INFORMATION: If the source cannot be identified, correct owned properties
and monitor.
HUMAN REVIEW: The brand facts owner approves and sends correction requests.
Expected output
A ranked source list with corrections, requests, and a recheck date.
Quality checklist
Source-level correction only; owned properties first; recheck scheduled.
Failure conditions
Corrects owned sources and monitors when the origin is unclear.

PR-83
PR-83  AI-ready content brief
 LLM AND AI SEO · INTERMEDIATE
When to use
When a citation gap indicates content is the missing piece.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to produce thin pages optimized only for extraction.
Required inputs
The gap, the question being answered, and our evidence.
Databases used
AI Visibility Opportunities, Content Briefs, Question Bank
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are briefing content designed to be citable.
OBJECTIVE: Produce a brief where extractability is designed in, not retrofitted.
CONTEXT: The gap: [DESCRIBE with the prompts that revealed it]. The question:
[PASTE]. Our evidence: [PASTE]. Brand facts relevant: [PASTE].
CONSTRAINTS: The piece must be genuinely useful to a reader first. Specify which
passages must stand alone. Every fact must match the brand fact sheet. Do not
brief a page whose only purpose is extraction.
PROCESS: 1 State the question and the correct answer. 2 Specify the direct-answer
block. 3 Specify the supporting depth a reader needs. 4 Identify facts requiring
sheet consistency. 5 Specify sources for every figure.
OUTPUT: The brief with the direct-answer specification, the extractable passage
list, the fact consistency requirements, and the source requirements.
QUALITY CHECKS: Reader value first; extractable passages specified; fact
consistency required.
MISSING INFORMATION: Without our own evidence, note the piece would be a summary
of what already ranks.
HUMAN REVIEW: The editor approves before commissioning.
Expected output
A brief with direct-answer specification and fact consistency requirements.
Quality checklist
Reader value first; passages specified; facts checked against the sheet.
Failure conditions
Flags briefs that would only summarize existing content.

PR-84  Competitor recommendation analysis
 LLM AND AI SEO · ADVANCED
When to use
Quarterly, on recommendation prompts where competitors appear and you do not.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to speculate about ranking factors inside these systems.
Required inputs
Captured recommendation answers, competitor public presence, our own presence.
Databases used
AI Prompt Runs, AI Competitors, AI Visibility Opportunities
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are analysing why competitors are recommended and we are not.
OBJECTIVE: Identify observable differences we could legitimately close.
CONTEXT: Recommendation answers: [PASTE verbatim with assistant and date].
Competitors named: [LIST]. Their public presence: [PASTE what you observed]. Our
equivalent: [PASTE].
CONSTRAINTS: Describe observable differences only. Do not speculate about system
internals or ranking factors. Do not conclude a single factor caused a
recommendation. Report reasons only where the answer states them.
PROCESS: 1 Count which brands are named across the set. 2 Record any stated
reasons. 3 Compare public presence — coverage, listings, reviews, content depth,
pricing transparency. 4 Identify closable differences. 5 Rank by effort and
frequency.
OUTPUT: A presence comparison table, the stated reasons, and a ranked list of
closable differences with effort.
QUALITY CHECKS: Observable differences only; no causal claims; gaps are specific.
MISSING INFORMATION: Exclude competitors whose presence data was not supplied.
HUMAN REVIEW: Strategy decides which differences to close.
Expected output
A presence comparison with stated reasons and ranked closable differences.
Quality checklist
Observable only; no causal or ranking-factor speculation.
Failure conditions
Excludes competitors without supplied presence data.

PR-85  Monthly performance analysis
 MEASUREMENT · INTERMEDIATE
When to use
Monthly, before the report is written.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to explain movement the data cannot account for.
Required inputs
All metrics with sources and as-of dates, plus the previous month.
Databases used
Reports, Business Goals, Content, Keyword Master
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are analysing a month of SEO performance.
OBJECTIVE: Separate what the data shows from what is being inferred.
CONTEXT: This month's metrics: [PASTE with sources and as-of dates]. Previous
month: [PASTE]. What we changed and when: [PASTE]. Quarterly goals: [PASTE].
CONSTRAINTS: Every figure needs a source and a date. Do not attribute a change to
a cause the data cannot support — list hypotheses instead. Report sample sizes
with every rate. Do not compare to industry benchmarks.
PROCESS: 1 Report progress against each goal. 2 Report month-on-month change per
metric. 3 Identify what changed in the same period. 4 Separate observation from
inference. 5 List hypotheses for unexplained movement.
OUTPUT: A goal progress table, a metric change table with sample sizes, the
observations, and the hypotheses labelled as hypotheses.
QUALITY CHECKS: Sources and dates on every figure; hypotheses labelled; no
benchmark comparison.
MISSING INFORMATION: Mark unavailable figures explicitly rather than dropping the
row.
HUMAN REVIEW: The SEO lead interprets before it becomes a report.
Expected output
Goal and metric tables with observations and labelled hypotheses.
Quality checklist
Sourced and dated; hypotheses labelled; no benchmarks.
Failure conditions
Marks unavailable figures rather than dropping rows.

PR-86  Executive SEO summary
 REPORTING · INTERMEDIATE
When to use
Monthly or quarterly, for an audience with no SEO context.
When NOT to use
Do not use it to present activity volume as achievement.
Required inputs
The performance analysis, the goals, and the decisions required.
Databases used
Reports, Business Goals, Decisions & Change Log
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are writing an executive summary of SEO performance.
OBJECTIVE: Tell a business audience what changed, what it means, and what you need.
CONTEXT: Performance analysis: [PASTE]. Business goals: [PASTE]. Decisions
required: [LIST]. Audience: [ROLES and what they care about].
CONSTRAINTS: Under 300 words. No SEO jargon without a plain-language equivalent.
Do not report activity volume. Do not forecast. Every figure carries its as-of
date. End with what you need from them.
PROCESS: 1 State progress against the business goal in their terms. 2 State what
changed and the leading hypothesis. 3 State the risk that matters most. 4 State
the decisions required and their consequence. 5 State what you need.
OUTPUT: The summary under 300 words, and a one-slide version of the same content.
QUALITY CHECKS: No jargon; no activity metrics; no forecasts; ends in a specific
ask.
MISSING INFORMATION: Where a business metric is unavailable, say so plainly rather
than substituting an SEO proxy.
HUMAN REVIEW: The SEO lead owns every number presented.
Expected output
A sub-300-word summary and a one-slide version.
Quality checklist
Plain language; no activity metrics or forecasts; ends in an ask.
Failure conditions
States unavailable business metrics rather than substituting proxies.

PR-87  System governance audit
 QA AND GOVERNANCE · ADVANCED
When to use
Quarterly, on the workspace itself rather than on the site.
When NOT to use
Do not use it as a performance review of people — it audits the system.
Required inputs
Database usage data, SOP run history, prompt usage, and ownership records.
Databases used
SOP Library, Prompt Library, all databases
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are auditing whether the SEO operating system is actually being used.
OBJECTIVE: Find the parts that are decorative and the parts that are load-bearing.
CONTEXT: Database record counts and last-modified dates: [PASTE]. SOP last-run
dates: [PASTE]. Prompt usage counts: [PASTE]. Ownership assignments: [PASTE].
CONSTRAINTS: Judge the system, not the people. A database nobody updates is a
design problem before it is a discipline problem. Recommend removal as readily as
enforcement.
PROCESS: 1 Identify databases with no activity in 60 days. 2 Identify SOPs never
run. 3 Identify prompts never used. 4 Identify records with no owner. 5 For each,
decide: enforce, simplify, or remove.
OUTPUT: An activity audit, the unowned records, and a decision per unused asset —
enforce, simplify, or remove.
QUALITY CHECKS: Removal is offered as readily as enforcement; the audit targets
the system rather than individuals.
MISSING INFORMATION: Where usage data is unavailable, note which assets cannot be
assessed.
HUMAN REVIEW: The SEO lead decides what to remove; removals are logged.
Expected output
An activity audit with an enforce, simplify, or remove decision per asset.
Quality checklist
System-focused; removal offered readily; unowned records surfaced.
Failure conditions
Names assets that cannot be assessed without usage data.

SECTION 13 · SOP LIBRARY
The SOP library, continued
SOP-01 to SOP-16 appear later in this document in their original form. SOP-17 to SOP-42 complete the
library, covering keywords, clusters, authority, local, AI visibility, reporting, and governance.
Forty-two procedures in total. Each carries an ID, an owner, a trigger, inputs, tools, preconditions, steps, an output, a quality gate,
failure conditions, an escalation route, a handoff, a cadence, a related database, a related prompt, and an example run. Build them
as one SOP database with these fields as properties.
SOP-17  Keyword scoring pass
 QUARTERLY
Purpose
Score every candidate against the ten criteria consistently.
Owner
SEO specialist
Trigger
Quarterly, or when 20+ new candidates accumulate
Inputs
Candidate keywords, demand evidence, current rankings, the scoring model
Tools
Keyword tool, Notion, SERP checks
Preconditions
The scoring weights are current and versioned
STEPS
1
Confirm the weight version has not changed since the last pass.
2
Score S1 to S10 for each candidate, leaving nothing blank.
3
Let the formula calculate the total and the band.
4
Review the distribution — if everything scores 60–70, the model is not discriminating.
5
Spot-check ten scores against a colleague’s independent scoring.
Output
A fully scored candidate set with bands assigned
Quality gate
No candidate has an unscored criterion; the distribution spreads across bands
Failure conditions
BLOCKED if weights changed mid-pass; INCOMPLETE if any criterion is blank
Escalation
If two people score the same keyword more than two points apart, revise the criterion definition
Handoff
To SOP-18 for mapping
Related database
Keyword Master
Related prompt
PR-15, PR-27
EXAMPLE RUN
A pass of 84 candidates produced 11 in Build now, 23 this quarter, and 14 declines; the distribution check flagged S6 as never varying, and its
definition was rewritten.
THE SOP LIBRARY
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai

SOP-18  Keyword-to-page mapping
 QUARTERLY
Purpose
Assign one owning page per keyword, or record a gap.
Owner
SEO specialist
Trigger
After every scoring pass
Inputs
Scored keywords, page inventory with topics, cluster plans
Tools
Notion, crawl export
Preconditions
Scoring is complete and the page inventory is current
STEPS
1
Match each scored keyword to the page whose intent it fits.
2
Flag keywords with no suitable page as content gaps.
3
Flag any page holding more than one primary keyword.
4
Resolve conflicts on intent, never on preference.
5
Record the mapping and update the cluster relation.
Output
A mapped keyword list with a gap list and resolved conflicts
Quality gate
No page holds two primary keywords
Failure conditions
BLOCKED if the page inventory is more than 30 days old
Escalation
Unresolvable conflicts go to the SEO lead with both options stated
Handoff
To SOP-19 if conflicts exist, otherwise to SOP-10
Related database
Keyword Master, Content
Related prompt
PR-43
EXAMPLE RUN
Mapping 84 keywords surfaced 6 pages holding two primaries each; four were resolved by differentiating intent and two by merging the pages.
SOP-19  Cannibalization review
 MONTHLY
Purpose
Resolve queries where two of our URLs alternate.
Owner
SEO specialist
Trigger
Monthly, and after any mapping pass
Inputs
90 days of query-level ranking URL history, internal link counts
Tools
Search Console, crawl export, Notion
Preconditions
Ranking history covers at least 90 days
STEPS
1
Export query-level ranking URLs for the period.
2
Identify queries where the ranking URL alternates.
3
Determine the intended owner by intent and conversion path.
4
Check internal links — they usually point at the wrong page.
5
Choose one resolution: consolidate, differentiate, de-optimize, or redirect.
6
Set a 60-day recheck and record it.
Output
A conflict list with one resolution and a recheck date each
Quality gate
Every conflict has a single chosen resolution and a recheck date
Failure conditions
INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE below 90 days of history — record a baseline instead
Escalation
Redirects and removals require SEO lead approval
Handoff
To SOP-22 for the internal link changes
Related database
Keyword Master, Content
Related prompt
PR-44
EXAMPLE RUN
Nine alternating queries; six resolved by de-optimizing the weaker page, two by merging, one deferred pending backlink review.

SOP-20  Topic cluster creation
 PER TOPIC
Purpose
Turn a prioritized topic into a build plan.
Owner
Content lead
Trigger
When a topic reaches This quarter priority
Inputs
Topic record, mapped keywords, existing coverage, commercial destination
Tools
Notion
Preconditions
Keyword mapping for the topic is complete
STEPS
1
Define the pillar scope and what it must cover.
2
Assign every mapped keyword to the pillar or a spoke.
3
Mark each spoke as new content or an update.
4
Specify bidirectional interlinking and the commercial path.
5
Create the cluster record with planned spoke count.
Output
A cluster record with a spoke list and interlinking specification
Quality gate
Every spoke has demand evidence and a commercial path exists
Failure conditions
BLOCKED without a commercial destination — the cluster would lead nowhere
Escalation
A cluster with no commercial destination goes to the SEO lead for a strategy decision
Handoff
To SOP-10 for briefing
Related database
Topic Clusters, Keyword Master
Related prompt
PR-46
EXAMPLE RUN
A nine-spoke cluster planned; four spokes were updates to existing pages rather than new commissions, which halved the estimated build time.
SOP-21  Content consolidation
 AS NEEDED
Purpose
Merge overlapping pages without losing equity.
Owner
Content lead
Trigger
When the cannibalization review or portfolio audit flags overlap
Inputs
Candidate pages, performance, backlinks, internal links
Tools
Crawl export, backlink tool, CMS
Preconditions
Backlink data is available for every candidate
STEPS
1
Identify the overlap precisely, section by section.
2
Choose the surviving URL on intent and equity.
3
List everything that must be carried across.
4
Specify redirects for every retired URL, one hop.
5
Update internal links to the surviving URL.
6
Recheck rankings and traffic after 60 days.
Output
A consolidated page, redirects live, and a recheck scheduled
Quality gate
Nothing in the retired pages was lost; every URL redirects one hop
Failure conditions
BLOCKED without backlink data on every candidate
Escalation
Any page with significant backlinks being retired goes to the SEO lead
Handoff
To SOP-22 for internal link updates
Related database
Content, Backlinks
Related prompt
PR-52
EXAMPLE RUN
Three thin pages merged into one; the surviving page recovered its previous combined traffic within seven weeks, verified at the 60-day recheck.

SOP-22  Internal linking implementation
 WEEKLY
Purpose
Turn scored opportunities into live, verified links.
Owner
Editor
Trigger
Weekly, from the approved queue
Inputs

Tools
CMS, Notion
Preconditions
Opportunities have passed relevance scoring
STEPS
1
Take the approved queue in priority order.
2
Implement each link with the specified anchor.
3
Confirm the link is live and points at the correct URL.
4
Update the opportunity record to Verified.
5
Recheck the target page inbound count.
Output
Live, verified internal links with updated records
Quality gate
Every implemented link is confirmed live before the record is closed
Failure conditions
INCOMPLETE if a link is marked implemented without a live check
Escalation
If a CMS limitation prevents implementation, log it as a technical issue
Handoff

SOP-23  Orphan page resolution
 MONTHLY
Purpose
Decide and act on pages reachable only from the sitemap.
Owner
SEO specialist
Trigger
Monthly, from the crawl
Inputs
Orphan list with traffic, backlinks, topic, and age
Tools
Crawler, backlink tool, CMS
Preconditions
A full crawl has completed within 7 days
STEPS
1
Classify each orphan as valuable, marginal, or obsolete.
2
For valuable pages, identify relevant source pages and queue the links.
3
For marginal pages, assess merge candidates.
4
For obsolete pages, specify a redirect destination.
5
Check backlinks before any removal.
Output
A decision and action per orphan page
Quality gate
No page is removed without a backlink check and a redirect
Failure conditions
BLOCKED on removal decisions without backlink data
Escalation
Removals of pages with backlinks require SEO lead approval
Handoff
To SOP-22 or SOP-21 depending on the decision
Related database

SOP-25  Digital PR campaign run

SOP-28  Toxic link review
 AS NEEDED
Purpose
Assess whether disavowal is genuinely warranted.
Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
Only on a manual action or a clear manipulative pattern
Inputs
Link profile, the suspected pattern, any manual action notice
Tools
Backlink tool, Search Console
Preconditions
A specific problem has been identified
STEPS
1
Establish the pattern and its scale.
2
Determine origin — built, inherited, or organic.
3
Attempt removal first and document each attempt.
4
Only then consider disavowal.
5
Record the reasoning before filing anything.
Output
A documented decision, which may be to take no action
Quality gate
Removal attempted and documented before any disavowal
Failure conditions
BLOCKED without evidence of an actual ranking or manual action problem
Escalation
Disavowal requires SEO lead approval and a logged decision
Handoff
To SOP-42 for the decision log
Related database
Backlinks, Decisions & Change Log
Related prompt
PR-75
EXAMPLE RUN
A pattern of 40 low-quality links was assessed; origin was an inherited agency campaign, removal succeeded on 11, and the decision was
recorded not to disavow the remainder.
SOP-29  Google Business Profile maintenance
 MONTHLY
Purpose
Keep every profile complete and consistent.
Owner
Local SEO owner
Trigger
Monthly, per location
Inputs
Canonical NAP, current profile contents, category research
Tools
Profile dashboard, Notion
Preconditions
The canonical NAP record exists and is confirmed
STEPS
1
Compare every profile field against the canonical record.
2
Assess category selection against how customers search.
3
Complete services, products, hours, and attributes.
4
Check the description against the brand fact sheet.
5
Record discrepancies and assign corrections.
Output
A corrected, complete profile and a discrepancy log
Quality gate
Every difference is quoted exactly with its field named
Failure conditions
BLOCKED if the canonical NAP has not been confirmed by the location owner
Escalation
A category change affecting visibility goes to the SEO lead first
Handoff
To SOP-30 for citations
Related database
Local Locations

SOP-30  Local citation audit
 MONTHLY
Purpose
Find and correct NAP drift across directories.
Owner
Local SEO owner
Trigger
Monthly, per location
Inputs
Canonical NAP, the citation list, directory access
Tools
Citation tool or manual checks, Notion
Preconditions
The canonical NAP is confirmed
STEPS
1
Check each listing character by character against the canonical record.
2
Quote every difference exactly, with its source.
3
Claim any unclaimed listing where possible.
4
Request corrections on listings we do not control.
5
Identify and merge duplicates.
6
Update the consistency rollup.
Output
A corrected citation set and a tracked correction queue
Quality gate
Consistency is measured character by character, not by impression
Failure conditions
INCOMPLETE if any listing is marked correct without an exact comparison
Escalation
Directories refusing correction are logged and noted in the AI visibility review
Handoff
To SOP-39
Related database

PR-76

Of 22 listings, 19 matched; two had an abbreviated street type and one carried a phone number from before a 2024 change.
SOP-31  Review response
 CONTINUOUS
Purpose
Respond to every review within the agreed window.
Owner
Local SEO owner
Trigger
Within 48 hours of any new review
Inputs
The review, the response policy, and internal context where available
Tools
Profile dashboard
Preconditions
A response policy exists
STEPS
1
Read the review and identify what was specifically said.
2
Choose the response type from the policy.
3
Acknowledge specifically, never generically.
4
Move any dispute offline.
5
Escalate anything indicating a service failure.
Output
A sent response and an internal escalation where warranted
Quality gate
No two responses use identical wording; no facts disputed publicly
Failure conditions
NEEDS HUMAN REVIEW on any review alleging a serious service failure
Escalation
Serious allegations go to the location owner immediately, before responding
Handoff
To SOP-39 for reporting

SOP-32  Local landing page build
 PER LOCATION
Purpose

Owner
Content lead
Trigger
When a location is added or a page audit finds duplication
Inputs

Tools

Preconditions
At least three genuinely local elements exist
STEPS
1
Confirm three or more genuinely local elements exist.
2
Build the page from the location template.
3
Match the address and hours to the canonical record exactly.
4
Add local schema matching the profile.
5
Add internal links from the site to the page.
6
Verify the page renders and the schema validates.
Output
A live, distinct location page with valid schema
Quality gate
Three or more genuine local elements; NAP matches exactly

SOP-33  Prompt universe creation
 ONCE, REVIEWED QUARTERLY
Purpose
Build the fixed tracking set, once.
Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
Once at setup, reviewed quarterly
Inputs
ICP decisions, question bank, competitor list
Tools
Notion, assistant access
Preconditions
The question bank contains real customer questions
STEPS
1
List the decisions where we should be a candidate.
2
Write conversational prompts for each, in speech form.
3
Add category, brand, competitor, comparison, and problem prompts.
4
Cap the set at 40 unless capacity justifies more.
5
Define what a good result looks like per prompt.
6
Lock the wording and record the version.
Output
A locked, typed prompt set with good-result definitions
Quality gate
All six prompt families represented; wording locked before the first run
Failure conditions
BLOCKED without a real question bank — problem prompts would be invented
Escalation
Any wording change creates a new record at version+1, never an edit
Handoff
To SOP-34 for the first run
Related database
AI Prompt Universe

SOP-34  Monthly AI visibility run
 MONTHLY
Purpose
Execute the tracking set identically every month.
Owner
AI visibility owner
Trigger
First week of each month
Inputs
The locked prompt universe, the brand fact sheet, assistant access
Tools
Assistant access, Notion
Preconditions
The prompt set has not changed since the last run
STEPS
1
Confirm no prompt wording has changed.
2
Run every core prompt on every tracked assistant.
3
Paste each answer verbatim into a run record.
4
Record mention and citation separately.
5
Compare every statement about us against the fact sheet.
6
Log factual errors as the highest-priority findings.
Output
A complete dated run set with classifications
Quality gate
Prompt wording matches the universe records exactly
Failure conditions
VOID any run where the wording differed; INCOMPLETE if a core prompt was skipped
Escalation
Factual errors escalate to the brand facts owner the same day
Handoff
To SOP-35 for scoring
Related database
AI Prompt Runs, Brand Facts
Related prompt
PR-79
EXAMPLE RUN
Thirty-eight prompts across three assistants produced 114 run records; two factual errors were found and escalated within the hour.
SOP-35  AI scorecard calculation
 MONTHLY
Purpose

AI visibility owner
Trigger
Immediately after the monthly run

Tools
Notion
Preconditions
Every core prompt has been run
STEPS
1
Confirm every core prompt was run.
2
Calculate the five measures.
3
Compare against the previous month.
4
Check whether the prompt set or assistant list changed.
5
Flag the month as comparable or not.
6
Record hypotheses for movement, labelled as hypotheses.
Output
A dated scorecard with five measures and a comparability flag
Quality gate
Set size and assistant list stated alongside every figure
Failure conditions
PROVISIONAL if any core prompt was skipped
Escalation
A set change is flagged to leadership before the figures are presented
Handoff
To SOP-40 for reporting

SOP-36  Brand fact correction
 AS NEEDED
Purpose
Correct an incorrect fact at every source that carries it.
Owner
Marketing owner
Trigger
Immediately on any factual error in a tracked answer
Inputs
The error quoted, the canonical fact, and every public source
Tools
CMS, profile dashboards, email
Preconditions
The canonical fact sheet exists
STEPS
1
Quote the error exactly and identify the correct fact.
2
Search our own properties for the incorrect version.
3
Search third-party sources for it.
4
Correct owned properties immediately.
5
Request corrections from third parties, in writing.
6
Set a recheck date on the next tracking run.
Output
Corrected owned sources and a tracked third-party request list
Quality gate
Owned properties corrected before any external request is sent
Failure conditions
BLOCKED from attempting to influence a model directly — corrections happen at source
Escalation
A third party refusing correction is logged and noted in reporting
Handoff
To SOP-34 for the recheck
Related database
Brand Facts, AI Prompt Runs
Related prompt
PR-82
EXAMPLE RUN
An outdated category description was found on our own about page and two directories; all three were corrected and the next run showed the
correct description.
SOP-37  AI citation gap analysis
 MONTHLY
Purpose
Convert observed source gaps into outreach work.
Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
Monthly, after the run
Inputs
Sources cited across runs, our presence in each, our assets
Tools
Notion
Preconditions
The monthly run is complete
STEPS
1
List every source cited across the run set with its frequency.
2
Identify which mention us and which mention competitors only.
3
Assess closability for each gap.
4
Specify a route per closable gap.
5
Rank by frequency and effort.
6
Create authority targets for the top gaps.
Output
A ranked gap list with routes and created outreach targets
Quality gate
Every entry has a specific route; nothing requiring payment is included
Failure conditions
INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE without frequency data — rank on relevance and say so
Escalation
A gap requiring a paid placement is declined and recorded
Handoff
To SOP-25 for outreach
Related database
AI Citations & Sources, Authority Targets
Related prompt
PR-81
EXAMPLE RUN
Eleven sources appeared more than twice; four covered competitors and not us, of which two had a realistic editorial route.

SOP-38  Competitor recommendation review
 QUARTERLY
Purpose

Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
Quarterly, on recommendation prompts
Inputs
Captured recommendation answers, competitor presence data
Tools
Notion
Preconditions
At least three months of run data exists
STEPS
1
Count which brands are named across recommendation prompts.
2
Record any reasons the answers state.
3
Compare public presence across observable dimensions.
4
Identify differences we could legitimately close.
5
Rank by effort and frequency.
Output
A presence comparison and a ranked list of closable differences
Quality gate
Only observable differences recorded; no ranking-factor speculation
Failure conditions
INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE below three months of runs
Escalation
Strategy decides which differences to close; nothing is actioned automatically
Handoff
To SOP-01 for the strategy review

SOP-39  Weekly reporting
 WEEKLY
Purpose
Turn the week into three decisions.
Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
Every Friday
Inputs
All performance data with sources and dates, last week’s report
Tools
Notion, analytics, Search Console
Preconditions
Last week’s report exists
STEPS
1
Assemble every figure with its source and as-of date.
2
Compare against last week and the quarterly goal.
3
Separate observation from inference.
4
State the three decisions the week requires.
5
Record the system changes each decision implies.
Output
A report under 500 words ending in three owned decisions
Quality gate
Every figure sourced and dated; the report ends in decisions
Failure conditions
INCOMPLETE if any figure lacks a source or an as-of date
Escalation
A decision with no owner is reassigned before the report is filed
Handoff
To SOP-42 for the decision log

 MONTHLY

Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
First week of each month

Tools

Preconditions
The monthly analysis and AI scorecard are complete
STEPS
1
Assemble the analysis, goals, and scorecard.

Write the executive summary under 300 words.

State the three decisions and their consequence.
5
End with what is needed from the audience.

No jargon, no activity metrics, no forecasts
Failure conditions

SOP-41  Experiment design and evaluation
 PER EXPERIMENT
Purpose
Change one thing and know whether it worked.
Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
When the team disagrees about what would work
Inputs
The hypothesis, a dated baseline, available volume
Tools
Notion, analytics
Preconditions
A baseline has been captured before any change
STEPS
1
Record the baseline with source and date, before anything changes.
2
Define exactly one variable and what stays constant.
3
Set the minimum sample and the evaluation date.
4
Write the decision rule before starting.
5
Run without interference until the evaluation date.
6
Apply the rule and record the outcome either way.
Output
A concluded experiment with an adopt, reject, or extend decision
Quality gate
The decision rule predates the result; one variable only
Failure conditions
BLOCKED without a baseline; INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE if volume cannot support a conclusion
Escalation
If volume is too low, run structured interviews instead and record that choice
Handoff
To SOP-42 for the decision log
Related database
Experiments, Decisions & Change Log
Related prompt
PR-99 equivalent — see Section 12
EXAMPLE RUN
An extractability experiment on twelve pages ran three monthly cycles; the rule was met and the change was adopted across the cluster.

SOP-42  Decision log update
 CONTINUOUS
Purpose
Record what was decided and why, while the reasoning is fresh.
Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
On every material decision
Inputs
The decision, its context, evidence, and expected outcome
Tools
Notion
Preconditions
The decision has actually been made
STEPS
1
Record the decision and the date.
2
Record the context as it was at the time.
3
Record the evidence and the options rejected.
4
Write a falsifiable expected outcome.
5
Set a review date.
6
On the review date, record the actual outcome and a verdict.
Output
A logged decision with a falsifiable prediction and a review date
Quality gate
The prediction is specific enough to be shown wrong
Failure conditions
INCOMPLETE without evidence or a review date
Escalation
A decision nobody will own is escalated rather than logged
Handoff
Feeds SOP-01 at the quarterly review
Related database
Decisions & Change Log
Related prompt
PR-87
EXAMPLE RUN
A decision to stop pursuing free-tier keywords was logged with the segment analysis as evidence and a 90-day review date; the verdict at review
was Correct.

SECTION 14 · TEMPLATES
The template library
Fifty-five reusable documents. Nobody in the team should write anything from a blank page — every
document type in the system has a starting structure with a completion criterion.
Each template below names the fields it contains, the criterion that says it is finished, the SOP and prompt that produce it, and the
database it lands in. Build them as Notion database templates so a new record starts complete rather than empty.

SOP-42 / PR-87
Decisions & Change
Log
BUILD TEMPLATES AS DATABASE TEMPLATES, NOT PAGES
A template stored as a page gets duplicated, edited, and forked. A Notion database template applies automatically when a record is
created, carries its own checklist inside the record, and cannot drift out of sync with the database it belongs to. The five-minute difference
in setup is the difference between a system people use and a folder of documents they forget.
THE TEMPLATE LIBRARY

SECTION 15 · WORKED EXAMPLE
Northstar Analytics: one opportunity, end to
end
A fictional B2B analytics SaaS company, from a market signal to a quarterly strategy change. Every value
shown is invented to demonstrate the shape of a completed record.
NORTHSTAR ANALYTICS IS FICTIONAL
Northstar Analytics does not exist. It is an invented B2B analytics SaaS company selling to data teams at mid-market software businesses.
Every figure, URL, keyword, score, and outcome shown for it is a placeholder written to show what a completed record looks like — none
of it is a claim about anything real, and none of it should be read as a benchmark.

WHAT THE SIXTEEN STEPS SHOW
Every record traces backwards to a customer statement and forwards to a commercial decision. The keyword exists because seven people
described a problem. The AI opportunity exists because three tracked prompts showed absence. The quarterly decision exists because the
monthly report ended in decisions rather than observations. That traceability is the point of the whole system — not the individual
records.
THE WORKED EXAMPLE
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai
Page 168 / 222

SECTION 16 · WORKFLOWS
Ten cross-functional workflows
Each one crosses several operating areas. These are what the databases exist to support — the
individual records are only useful because these sequences run through them.
WORKFLOW 01  Customer problem to published content
 W-01
Trigger
A validated problem reaches three distinct people
Databases touched
Customer Problems → Search Demand → Keyword Master → Topic Clusters → Content Briefs → Content
Status changes
New → Validated → Mapped → Brief → Drafting → Review → Published
SOPs used
SOP-03, SOP-17, SOP-18, SOP-20, SOP-10, SOP-13
Prompts used
PR-31, PR-36, PR-43, PR-46, PR-06, PR-62
Human owners
Content lead owns the sequence; the editor owns the QA gate
Quality gates
Demand validation pack, keyword mapping, brief approval, content QA
Final output
A published page answering an evidenced problem
Handoff
To internal linking (W-05) and AI visibility tracking
Learning recorded
The angle that worked and the coverage questions readers still asked
WORKFLOW 02  Keyword opportunity to mapped page
 W-02
Trigger
A keyword scores above 60 and has no owning page
Databases touched
Keyword Master → Content → Topic Clusters → Content Briefs
Status changes
Unmapped → Mapped → Brief → Published
SOPs used
SOP-17, SOP-18, SOP-19, SOP-10
Prompts used
PR-15, PR-43, PR-44, PR-06
Human owners
SEO specialist maps; content lead commissions
Quality gates
All ten criteria scored; no page holds two primaries
Final output
A keyword with exactly one owning page
Handoff
To content production (W-01)
Learning recorded
Whether the mapping held or produced cannibalization at 60 days
WORKFLOW 03  Declining page to recovered asset
 W-03
Trigger
The decay monitor flags a page declining over 90 days
Databases touched
Content → Content Refresh Queue → Content Briefs → Content
Status changes
Declining → Diagnosed → Scheduled → Shipped → Verified
SOPs used
SOP-09, SOP-14, SOP-13
Prompts used
PR-16, PR-55, PR-62
Human owners
Content lead diagnoses; the writer executes; the editor QAs
Quality gates
Cause type identified before an action; 60-day recovery verification
Final output
An updated page with a verified recovery outcome
Handoff
To reporting (W-10) with the recovery result
Learning recorded
Which cause types actually recover and which do not
END-TO-END WORKFLOWS
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai
Page 169 / 222

WORKFLOW 04  Technical issue to verified fix
 W-04
Trigger
A crawl, alert, or log review surfaces an issue
Databases touched
Technical Issues → Technical Releases & Verification → Site Architecture
Status changes
Found → Diagnosed → Ticketed → In progress → Deployed → Verified
SOPs used
SOP-06, SOP-07, SOP-20
Prompts used
PR-13, PR-14, PR-64, PR-65
Human owners
Technical SEO owner diagnoses; engineering owner ships; both verify
Quality gates
Confidence above 3 before ticketing; production verification before closure
Final output
A verified fix with a recorded date
Handoff
To release verification and the 30-day index check
Learning recorded
Whether the issue recurs after the next release
WORKFLOW 05  Orphan money page to supported architecture
 W-05
Trigger
A crawl shows a commercial page with fewer than five inbound internal links
Databases touched
Content → Internal Link Opportunities → Site Architecture
Status changes
Identified → Approved → Implemented → Verified
SOPs used
SOP-22, SOP-23
Prompts used
PR-68, PR-69, PR-70
Human owners
SEO specialist identifies; the editor implements and verifies
Quality gates
Relevance above 3; every link carries a reader rationale
Final output
A commercial page with meaningful internal support
Handoff
To reporting, with the inbound count before and after
Learning recorded
Which source pages actually drive the most qualified onward clicks
WORKFLOW 06  Original data to earned citations
 W-06
Trigger
First-party data exists with publication permission
Databases touched
Content → Digital PR Campaigns → Authority Targets → Backlinks → AI Citations & Sources
Status changes
Planning → Live → Closed
SOPs used
SOP-24, SOP-25, SOP-27
Prompts used
PR-49, PR-73, PR-74
Human owners
Marketing owner runs the campaign; the data owner clears publication
Quality gates
Methodology published; no claim exceeds the data; permissions in writing
Final output
Earned coverage, links, and citations logged against effort
Handoff
To AI visibility, where cited sources are tracked
Learning recorded
Which asset types and angles actually earn coverage
END-TO-END WORKFLOWS
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai
Page 170 / 222

WORKFLOW 07  Local inconsistency to corrected presence
 W-07
Trigger
A monthly audit finds a listing that does not match the canonical record
Databases touched
Local Locations → Local Citations → Content
Status changes
Found → Inconsistent → Correction requested → Correct
SOPs used
SOP-29, SOP-30, SOP-32
Prompts used
PR-76, PR-78
Human owners
Local SEO owner audits and requests; the location owner confirms the canonical NAP
Quality gates
Character-level comparison; differences quoted exactly
Final output
A consistent NAP across every listing
Handoff
To reporting, with the consistency percentage
Learning recorded
Which directories drift most often and how quickly they respond
WORKFLOW 08  AI prompt gap to content opportunity
 W-08
Trigger
A tracked prompt shows absence across three consecutive runs
Databases touched
AI Prompt Runs → AI Visibility Opportunities → Content Briefs → Content
Status changes
Identified → Scheduled → In progress → Done
SOPs used
SOP-34, SOP-37, SOP-10
Prompts used
PR-79, PR-81, PR-83
Human owners
AI visibility owner identifies; content lead schedules and delivers
Quality gates
Linked to the evidencing runs; reader value before extractability
Final output
Published content designed to be both useful and citable
Handoff
To the next monthly run, where the gap is rechecked
Learning recorded
Whether the content changed the mention or citation outcome
WORKFLOW 09  Factual AI error to corrected source ecosystem
 W-09
Trigger
A tracked answer states something incorrect about the brand
Databases touched
AI Prompt Runs → Brand Facts → Content → Local Citations → AI Prompt Runs
Status changes
Error found → Sources identified → Owned corrected → Requests sent → Rechecked
SOPs used
SOP-34, SOP-36, SOP-30
Prompts used
PR-79, PR-82, PR-21
Human owners
Marketing owner corrects at source; AI visibility owner rechecks
Quality gates
Owned properties corrected first; no attempt to influence the model directly
Final output
A corrected public source ecosystem and a rechecked answer
Handoff
To the next monthly run and the accuracy measure
Learning recorded
How long correction takes to appear, per source type
END-TO-END WORKFLOWS
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai
Page 171 / 222

WORKFLOW 10  Monthly performance data to quarterly strategy change
 W-10
Trigger
The monthly reporting cycle completes
Databases touched
Reports → Decisions & Change Log → SEO Strategy → Business Goals
Status changes
Analysed → Decisions assigned → Strategy revised → Bets set
SOPs used
SOP-39, SOP-40, SOP-01, SOP-42
Prompts used
PR-85, PR-86, PR-30, PR-87
Human owners
SEO lead analyses and proposes; the business owner approves the strategy
Quality gates
Every figure sourced and dated; the stated-versus-actual gap named
Final output
A versioned strategy with three new bets and a not-doing list
Handoff
To the next quarter, where the cycle restarts at W-01
Learning recorded
Whether the previous quarter’s predictions proved correct
INSTRUMENT THREE, NOT TEN
W-01, W-04, and W-10 together cover demand becoming content, findings becoming shipped fixes, and data becoming strategy. If those
three run reliably, the other seven are variations on patterns the team already knows. Trying to instrument all ten in the first month
produces ten half-followed processes.
END-TO-END WORKFLOWS
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai

Ten waves. Each wave relates only to databases already created.
WAVE
DATABASES
WHY THIS ORDER
First
Business Goals, SEO Strategy
Nothing else has anything to point at without these
Second
Customer Problems, Voice of Customer, Search Demand, Question Bank
The demand layer feeds keywords and content
Third
Keyword Master, Query Patterns, Declined Keywords
Keywords relate to demand records already created
Fourth
Topics, Topic Clusters, Content, Content Briefs, Content Refresh Queue
Content relates to keywords and clusters, which must exist first
Fifth
Site Architecture, Technical Issues, Technical Releases & Verification,
Internal Link Opportunities
These relate to Content, which is now in place
Sixth
Backlinks, Authority Targets, Digital PR Campaigns
Authority relates to Content assets
Seventh
Local Locations, Local Citations
Independent of the rest; build only if local applies
Eighth
Brand Facts, AI Prompt Universe, AI Prompt Runs, AI Citations & Sources, AI
Competitors, AI Visibility Opportunities, Historical Scorecards
The AI layer relates to Content and Brand Facts, both now in place
Ninth
Experiments, Reports, Decisions & Change Log
These relate to everything, so they come last
Tenth
Prompt Library, SOP Library, Template Library
Reference databases with no dependencies — build them whenever
HOW TO BUILD IT
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai
Page 174 / 222

Three deployment modes
The same architecture, scaled to who is operating it.
Solo consultant or founder
Build
About 90 minutes. Build nine databases: Business Goals, Customer Problems, Keyword Master, Topics, Content, Technical Issues, Internal Link
Opportunities, Brand Facts, and Decisions & Change Log. Skip clusters, briefs, and the full AI layer until there is enough activity to justify them.
Run
Run SOP-02 weekly and SOP-39 on Fridays. Everything else waits.
COMMON MISTAKE
The temptation is to build all thirty-two and use four. Build nine and use nine.
Small marketing team
Build
About five hours across two sessions. Build all thirty-two databases but populate the demand, keyword, and content layers first. Assign owners
at the database level rather than the record level initially.
Run
Run the full weekly rhythm with a named owner per day. Add the AI layer in month two, once the content layer is producing.
COMMON MISTAKE
Distributing the message approval gate across the team, after which quality drifts within a fortnight.
Agency or multi-client team
Build
About nine hours plus per-client setup. Build the master workspace once, add a Client relation to every client-specific database, and keep the
SOP, Prompt, and Template libraries shared and unfiltered.
Run
Run per-client weekly rhythms offset across the week. Client-specific Brand Facts and voice files are non-negotiable.
COMMON MISTAKE
Sharing a brand fact sheet or voice file across clients, after which every client’s output starts sounding identical.
HOW TO BUILD IT
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai
Page 175 / 222

SECTION 19 · MULTI-CLIENT
The agency and multi-project model
One master workspace with a Client relation, shared libraries, and strict separation of anything
brand-specific. The alternative — one workspace per client — is defensible but does not scale past four.
DECISION
ONE WORKSPACE PER CLIENT
MASTER WORKSPACE WITH A CLIENT RELATION
Setup time
Multiplied by the number of clients
Once, plus about 20 minutes per client
SOP and prompt
updates
Repeated in every workspace, and they drift
Updated once, applied everywhere
Cross-client learning
Effectively impossible
A Client filter removed shows patterns across the portfolio
Data separation
Absolute by construction
Depends entirely on discipline with the Client property
Client access
Simple — share the workspace
Requires per-database permissions or a filtered client-facing page
Offboarding
Archive or transfer the whole workspace
Filter, export, and archive by Client — more work
Best for
Three or fewer clients, or where the contract requires
isolation
Four or more clients, and any team that wants shared learning
What must be client-specific, and what must not
Always client-specific
•
Brand Facts — a shared fact sheet is a data leak waiting to happen
•
Voice and style — sharing one is why agency output sounds identical
•
Business Goals and SEO Strategy
•
Every demand, keyword, content, and technical record
•
AI prompt universes and all run data
•
Reports, decisions, and experiment records
Always shared
•
The SOP Library — the procedures are your product
•
The Prompt Library — with client context pasted at run time
•
The Template Library
•
Query Patterns and the scoring model definitions
•
The technical module list and severity definitions
•
The workflow definitions in Section 16
Preventing data leakage between clients
Six rules. The first one prevents most of what goes wrong.
Every client-specific database has a required Client relation — no record without one
Every default view filters on Client; the unfiltered view is not the default
Prompt runs paste the client context explicitly; nothing relies on remembered context
Brand Facts and voice files are never shared, referenced, or copied between clients
Client-facing pages are separate filtered pages, never the working database
Offboarding filters by Client, exports, archives, then removes access — in that order
ON CLIENT CONFIDENTIALITY
Nothing in this playbook assumes you may use one client’s data to inform another’s work. Cross-client learning means procedural learning
— which SOPs work, which prompt phrasings produce usable output, which technical modules generate the most issues. It does not mean
sharing keyword lists, performance figures, brand facts, or research between accounts. Check your contracts before assuming otherwise.
THE MULTI-CLIENT MODEL
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai
Page 176 / 222

BUILD ORDER, IF YOU ARE STARTING FROM NOTHING
Keywords and Content first — those two alone replace most spreadsheets. Then Technical Issues and Internal Links, because that is where
the fastest wins usually are. Then AI Visibility, which needs a month of data before it says anything. Strategy, Experiments, and Reporting
last, once there is enough activity to review. Building all fifteen in week one produces fourteen empty databases and one you actually use.

IF YOU ONLY HAVE TWO HOURS A WEEK
Do Monday and Friday. Thirty minutes on Monday to look at what changed and pick one thing; ninety minutes on Friday to do that one
thing, record what happened, and pick the next. A two-hour rhythm sustained for a year beats a full programme abandoned in March,
and it is the version most solo founders and small teams actually manage.
