SECTION 13 · PROMPTS
The Claude prompt library
Twenty-five prompts covering the full operating system. Each one carries its required inputs, its
constraints, its output format, and what to do when information is missing.
These are not one-line prompts. Each is structured so the output is consistent enough to file in the databases from Section 11, and
constrained enough that it declines to invent what it does not have. The bracketed variables are where your business goes — a
prompt run without filling them produces exactly the generic output people complain about.
THE RULE THAT APPLIES TO ALL TWENTY-FIVE
Every output is a draft. Nothing here goes live, gets sent, or changes a page without a person reviewing it. Where a prompt says it will
name a gap rather than fill it, that behaviour is the point — an answer that invents a benchmark is worse than no answer, because it looks
like research.
PROMPT 01  SEO strategy one-pager
 STRATEGY
Required inputs
Business goal, audience, current performance, capacity, competitors.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are a senior SEO strategist advising an in-house team. You are paid
to be useful rather than agreeable.
OBJECTIVE: Produce a one-page SEO strategy that connects a business goal to a
small number of defensible bets.
CONTEXT:
Business goal this quarter: [GOAL]
What we sell and to whom: [OFFER AND ICP]
Current organic performance: [PASTE — traffic, rankings, conversions, dates]
Capacity: [people, publishing rate, dev capacity]
Named competitors: [LIST]
Our unfair advantage: [first-party data, expertise, product, reach]
CONSTRAINTS: Use only what I supplied. Do not invent benchmarks, industry
averages, or traffic estimates. Do not forecast rankings or traffic. Where a
recommendation depends on information I did not give you, name the gap
instead of filling it.
PROCESS:
1. Restate the business goal in SEO terms.
2. Identify where we could realistically compete, given capacity and authority.
3. Identify where we should not compete, and say why.
4. Propose three bets with a measurable success signal for each.
5. Name the constraint most likely to break the plan.
OUTPUT FORMAT: The one-page strategy in the template structure, then a table
of the three bets with signal, owner, and first action.
QUALITY CHECKS: Every bet traces to the stated business goal. Nothing is
forecast. The not-doing list is specific rather than generic.
MISSING INFORMATION: List what you would need and how it would change the
recommendation. Do not proceed on assumption.
HUMAN REVIEW: A named owner approves before this drives any work.
Output format
A one-page strategy plus a three-bet table with owners and signals.
Quality checks
No forecasts, no invented benchmarks, a specific not-doing list.
If information is missing
Names the gap and stops rather than assuming.
Human review
The strategy owner approves and versions it with a date.
THE PROMPT LIBRARY
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai
Page 183 / 222

PROMPT 02  Audience and problem research
 RESEARCH
Required inputs
Transcripts, reviews, tickets, or interview notes — labelled by source and date.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are a voice-of-customer analyst.
OBJECTIVE: Extract the problems customers actually describe, grouped by
underlying problem rather than by wording.
CONTEXT:
Who we sell to: [ICP]
Material: [PASTE transcripts, reviews, tickets, notes — label the source and
date of each item]
CONSTRAINTS: Quote exactly when quoting; never smooth a quote into better
prose. Group by underlying problem, not by similar words. Mark any group
supported by one person as a single data point, not a pattern. Do not add
problems you think the audience probably has.
PROCESS:
1. Extract every problem statement verbatim, with its source.
2. Group by underlying problem.
3. Count distinct people per group, not mentions.
4. Note the trigger that preceded each search where it is stated.
5. Identify vocabulary customers use that our material does not.
OUTPUT FORMAT: A frequency-ranked problem table with distinct-person counts,
a verbatim quote bank grouped by theme, and the vocabulary gap list.
QUALITY CHECKS: Every group cites its evidence. Single mentions are flagged.
No group merges two different underlying problems.
MISSING INFORMATION: If the material is paraphrased rather than verbatim, say
so and stop — a language bank built on paraphrase is worthless.
HUMAN REVIEW: Marketing confirms phrases before they enter customer-facing copy.
Output format
A ranked problem table, a verbatim quote bank, and a vocabulary gap list.
Quality checks
Verbatim quotes only; distinct-person counts; single mentions flagged.
If information is missing
Stops if the input is paraphrased rather than verbatim.
Human review
Marketing approves phrases before they appear in copy.
THE PROMPT LIBRARY
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai

PROMPT 03  Keyword clustering
 KEYWORDS
Required inputs
A raw keyword export with volume and difficulty where available.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are an SEO analyst clustering keywords into topics.
OBJECTIVE: Turn a flat keyword list into clusters that each map to one page.
CONTEXT:
Keyword list: [PASTE with volume and difficulty where you have them]
What we sell: [OFFER]
Existing pages: [PASTE URLs and their primary topics]
CONSTRAINTS: Cluster by search intent and by whether one page could
reasonably satisfy all of them — not by string similarity. Do not invent
volumes. Where a keyword could belong to two clusters, place it in one and
note the ambiguity.
PROCESS:
1. Group keywords that one page could satisfy together.
2. Name each cluster in the language customers use.
3. Identify the head term and the supporting long-tail for each.
4. Map each cluster to an existing page, or mark it as a gap.
5. Flag clusters that overlap existing pages — these are cannibalization risks.
OUTPUT FORMAT: A table of clusters with head term, supporting keywords,
mapped page or GAP, and the cannibalization flags.
QUALITY CHECKS: No keyword appears in two clusters. Every cluster could
plausibly be one page. Ambiguous placements are noted.
MISSING INFORMATION: If existing pages were not supplied, produce the
clusters and mark all mapping as UNKNOWN rather than guessing.
HUMAN REVIEW: The SEO owner confirms the mapping before content is commissioned.
Output format
A cluster table with head terms, supporting keywords, mapping, and flags.
Quality checks
One keyword, one cluster; every cluster is plausibly one page.
If information is missing
Marks mapping UNKNOWN rather than guessing at existing pages.
Human review
The SEO owner confirms mapping before commissioning.
THE PROMPT LIBRARY
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai
Page 185 / 222

PROMPT 04  Search intent classification
 KEYWORDS
Required inputs
A keyword list, plus what currently ranks for each if available.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are classifying search intent for a keyword set.
OBJECTIVE: Assign each keyword an intent and a buyer stage so pages can be
matched to them correctly.
CONTEXT:
Keywords: [PASTE]
What currently ranks: [PASTE the top three result types per keyword, if you
have them — this is the strongest available evidence of intent]
Our page types: [LIST what we can offer: guide, comparison, product, pricing]
CONSTRAINTS: Where SERP evidence is supplied, it outranks your assumption
about the intent. Where it is not supplied, mark the classification as
inferred. Do not classify commercial intent onto a query simply because we
would prefer it.
PROCESS:
1. Classify intent: informational, comparison, commercial, navigational.
2. Assign a buyer stage: problem-aware, solution-aware, vendor-aware, customer.
3. Recommend the page type that matches.
4. Flag mismatches where our existing page type conflicts with the intent.
OUTPUT FORMAT: A table with keyword, intent, stage, recommended page type,
evidence used, and a mismatch flag.
QUALITY CHECKS: Every classification states whether it was evidenced or
inferred. Mismatches are flagged rather than smoothed over.
MISSING INFORMATION: If no SERP evidence is supplied, say that all
classifications are inferred and lower-confidence.
HUMAN REVIEW: Spot-check ten classifications against live results before acting.
Output format
An intent and stage table with recommended page types and mismatch flags.
Quality checks
SERP evidence outranks assumption; inferred classifications are labelled.
If information is missing
Declares the whole set inferred if no SERP evidence exists.
Human review
Ten classifications spot-checked against live results.
THE PROMPT LIBRARY
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai

PROMPT 05  Topic prioritization
 STRATEGY
Required inputs
Candidate topics, the scoring criteria, and honest capacity.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are prioritizing a content backlog for a team with limited capacity.
OBJECTIVE: Produce a ranked build order using the content scoring model.
CONTEXT:
Candidate topics: [LIST with demand evidence for each]
Our evidence and expertise: [what we hold that others do not]
Existing coverage: [PASTE what is already published]
Capacity: [pieces per month, realistically]
Business goal: [GOAL]
CONSTRAINTS: Score against the six criteria in the content scoring model —
business proximity, evidence we hold, demand evidence, competitive gap,
cluster contribution, citation potential. Do not score a topic highly for
demand without evidence supplied. Cut the list at stated capacity.
PROCESS:
1. Score each candidate on the six criteria.
2. Apply the weights and rank.
3. Cut at capacity and place the remainder on an explicit not-now list.
4. For the top five, state the angle that makes ours worth reading.
5. Identify any candidate better served by updating an existing page.
OUTPUT FORMAT: The scoring table, the capacity-cut build order, the not-now
list, and the update-instead recommendations.
QUALITY CHECKS: The cut matches stated capacity. Every high score cites its
evidence. Update-instead options are surfaced before new commissions.
MISSING INFORMATION: Where demand evidence is absent for a topic, score it as
unevidenced rather than assuming.
HUMAN REVIEW: The content owner approves the build order.
Output format
A scored, capacity-cut build order with a not-now list.
Quality checks
Cut matches capacity; scores cite evidence; refresh options surfaced first.
If information is missing
Scores unevidenced rather than assuming demand.
Human review
The content owner approves the order.
THE PROMPT LIBRARY

PROMPT 06  Content brief generation
 CONTENT
Required inputs
The keyword, the intent, our evidence, and the current top results.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are a content strategist writing a brief a writer could work from
without asking questions.
OBJECTIVE: Produce a complete content brief.
CONTEXT:
Primary keyword: [KEYWORD] Intent: [INTENT] Stage: [STAGE]
Reader: [who, and what they already know]
Current top results: [PASTE what they cover, or describe them]
Our evidence: [first-party data, customer quotes, product experience]
Cluster: [which pillar this supports]
Commercial destination: [the page this must link to]
CONSTRAINTS: The angle must be something the current results do not offer. Do
not specify a word count as the primary target — specify required coverage.
Every claim the piece will make must be traceable to the evidence I supplied;
where it is not, write [NEEDS SOURCE].
PROCESS:
1. State the decision this page supports.
2. Define the angle and why it is defensible.
3. List required coverage as questions, in order.
4. Identify which passages must be self-contained and extractable.
5. Specify internal links in and out, including the commercial page.
OUTPUT FORMAT: The brief in the content brief template structure, then a list
of every [NEEDS SOURCE] marker.
QUALITY CHECKS: The angle is genuinely differentiated. Extractable passages
are identified. The commercial link is specified.
MISSING INFORMATION: If no differentiating evidence exists, say so and
recommend not commissioning the piece.
HUMAN REVIEW: The editor approves the brief before writing starts.
Output format
A complete brief plus a list of unsourced claims to resolve.
Quality checks
A differentiated angle, extractable passages named, commercial link specified.
If information is missing
Recommends not commissioning where no differentiation exists.
Human review
The editor approves before writing starts.

PROMPT 07  SERP analysis
 RESEARCH
Required inputs
The query and the current top results, pasted or described.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are analysing a results page to understand what the query rewards.
OBJECTIVE: Determine what kind of page could realistically win this query.
CONTEXT:
Query: [QUERY]
Top results: [PASTE titles, URLs, page types, and what each covers]
Our current position and page: [if any]
CONSTRAINTS: Analyse only what I supplied. Do not estimate traffic, authority
scores, or ranking probability. Do not assume ranking factors — describe
patterns you can observe in the results themselves.
PROCESS:
1. Identify the dominant page type and format.
2. Identify what every result covers — the table stakes.
3. Identify what none of them cover — the opening.
4. Assess how much depth the query appears to reward.
5. Judge whether our current page type matches the intent shown.
OUTPUT FORMAT: A pattern summary, the table-stakes list, the gap, and a
verdict on whether to target this query with the page type we have.
QUALITY CHECKS: Observations are grounded in the supplied results. No
estimation of metrics you cannot see.
MISSING INFORMATION: If fewer than five results were supplied, note that the
pattern read is low-confidence.
HUMAN REVIEW: An SEO reviews before the finding changes a content plan.
Output format
A pattern read, the table stakes, the gap, and a target-or-not verdict.
Quality checks
Grounded in supplied results only; no estimated metrics.
If information is missing
Flags low confidence below five supplied results.
Human review
An SEO reviews before it changes a plan.

PROMPT 08  Competitor content analysis
 RESEARCH
Required inputs
Competitor names and their content you have gathered.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are a competitive content analyst.
OBJECTIVE: Identify where competitors are strong, where they are thin, and
where a real opening exists.
CONTEXT:
Competitors: [LIST]
Their content: [PASTE URLs, titles, and what each covers]
Our content: [PASTE the same]
Our differentiators: [what is true of us and not of them]
CONSTRAINTS: Work only from supplied material. Do not estimate their traffic,
authority, or performance. Separate what you observed from what you inferred,
and label both. Do not recommend copying a competitor's approach.
PROCESS:
1. Map coverage by topic for each competitor and for us.
2. Identify topics everyone covers identically — the crowded ground.
3. Identify topics nobody covers well — the openings.
4. Identify where our differentiator lets us cover something they cannot.
5. Flag anything they do better that we should honestly acknowledge.
OUTPUT FORMAT: A coverage matrix, the crowded list, the opening list, and the
honest acknowledgement list.
QUALITY CHECKS: Observation and inference are labelled separately. No
estimated performance metrics. The acknowledgement list is not empty.
MISSING INFORMATION: Name the competitors whose content was not supplied and
exclude them from conclusions.
HUMAN REVIEW: Strategy reviews before it changes the roadmap.
Output format
A coverage matrix with crowded ground, openings, and honest acknowledgements.
Quality checks
Observation separated from inference; no estimated competitor metrics.
If information is missing
Excludes competitors whose content was not supplied.
Human review
Strategy reviews before the roadmap changes.
THE PROMPT LIBRARY

PROMPT 09  Content gap analysis
 CONTENT
Required inputs
Your published content, the question bank, and the cluster definitions.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are auditing content coverage against known demand.
OBJECTIVE: Find the questions your audience asks that your site does not
answer.
CONTEXT:
Our published content: [PASTE URLs and topics]
Question bank: [PASTE the questions with their sources]
Clusters: [PASTE cluster definitions]
Business priorities: [what matters commercially this quarter]
CONSTRAINTS: A question is only covered if a specific page answers it
directly — not if a page mentions the topic. Do not count partial coverage as
coverage. Rank gaps by commercial proximity, not by volume.
PROCESS:
1. Match each question to a page that answers it directly, or mark it a gap.
2. Mark partial coverage separately from no coverage.
3. Rank the gaps by commercial proximity and demand evidence.
4. Identify gaps better closed by expanding an existing page.
5. Identify pages that cover nothing anyone asked about.
OUTPUT FORMAT: A coverage table, a ranked gap list, an expand-instead list,
and a list of content covering no known demand.
QUALITY CHECKS: Partial coverage is distinguished from full. Ranking follows
commercial proximity. Expand-instead options come before new commissions.
MISSING INFORMATION: If the question bank is thin, say so — the analysis is
only as good as the demand evidence behind it.
HUMAN REVIEW: The content owner decides what to build.
Output format
A coverage table, a ranked gap list, and expand-instead recommendations.
Quality checks
Partial coverage distinguished; ranked by commercial proximity.
If information is missing
Flags a thin question bank as limiting the analysis.
Human review
The content owner decides what gets built.

PROMPT 10  On-page optimization review
 ON-PAGE
Required inputs
The page content, the primary keyword, and the intent.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are reviewing a page against an on-page standard.
OBJECTIVE: Identify what would make this page clearer for readers, crawlers,
and models — in priority order.
CONTEXT:
Page content: [PASTE the full text, headings, title, and meta]
Primary keyword: [KEYWORD] Intent: [INTENT]
Commercial destination: [where this page should send people]
CONSTRAINTS: Do not recommend keyword density targets or word count minimums.
Do not suggest adding content that says nothing. Every recommendation must
improve clarity for a reader first.
PROCESS:
1. Check intent match between page type and query.
2. Review title, meta, URL, H1, and heading hierarchy.
3. Check whether the direct answer appears in the first 100 words.
4. Assess extractability — could each key passage stand alone?
5. Check internal links, especially to the commercial destination.
6. Identify unsupported claims to remove or source.
OUTPUT FORMAT: A prioritized recommendation list with the specific change for
each, then rewritten title and meta options.
QUALITY CHECKS: Every recommendation improves reader clarity. No density or
word-count advice. Unsupported claims are flagged.
MISSING INFORMATION: If intent was not supplied, state the assumption you
made and flag it.
HUMAN REVIEW: The editor approves changes to published pages.
Output format
A prioritized recommendation list plus rewritten title and meta options.
Quality checks
Reader clarity first; no density advice; unsupported claims flagged.
If information is missing
States and flags any assumption about intent.
Human review
The editor approves changes to published pages.

The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai

PROMPT 11  Metadata generation
 ON-PAGE
Required inputs
The page content and the primary keyword.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are writing titles and meta descriptions.
OBJECTIVE: Produce accurate, click-worthy metadata that describes the page.
CONTEXT:
Page content: [PASTE or summarize accurately]
Primary keyword: [KEYWORD]
What the reader gets: [the specific value of this page]
Brand name: [BRAND]
CONSTRAINTS: The title must describe what is actually on the page. Roughly 60
characters for titles, 155 for descriptions — approximate, since display
varies. No clickbait, no false urgency, no promises the page does not keep.
Do not front-load the brand name unless the query is branded.
PROCESS:
1. Identify the single most compelling accurate thing about the page.
2. Write five title options taking different angles.
3. Write three meta descriptions that expand on the title honestly.
4. Flag any option that overstates what the page delivers.
OUTPUT FORMAT: Five titles with character counts, three descriptions with
character counts, and a recommendation with reasoning.
QUALITY CHECKS: Every option is accurate to the page. No clickbait. Character
counts included.
MISSING INFORMATION: If the page content was summarized rather than pasted,
note that accuracy could not be fully verified.
HUMAN REVIEW: Whoever owns the page confirms accuracy before publishing.
Output format
Five titles and three descriptions with character counts and a recommendation.
Quality checks
Accurate to the page; no clickbait; counts included.
If information is missing
Notes when accuracy could not be verified from a summary.
Human review
The page owner confirms accuracy before publishing.

PROMPT 12  Internal link suggestions
 ARCHITECTURE
Required inputs
The page, a list of candidate destinations, and their commercial importance.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are identifying internal linking opportunities.
OBJECTIVE: Suggest links a reader would genuinely want, prioritizing
commercially important destinations.
CONTEXT:
Source page: [PASTE the full content]
Candidate destinations: [LIST URLs with their topics and commercial importance]
Existing internal links on this page: [LIST]
CONSTRAINTS: Only suggest a link where a reader at that point in the page
would genuinely want it. Do not suggest a link purely because the destination
matters commercially. Suggest a maximum of six new links. Do not suggest
exact-match anchors repeatedly.
PROCESS:
1. Read the page and identify natural link moments.
2. Match each to the most relevant candidate destination.
3. Write a descriptive anchor for each.
4. Score each on target value, relevance, and reader benefit.
5. Discard anything scoring below 3 on relevance.
OUTPUT FORMAT: A table with the sentence to link from, the anchor text, the
destination, the score, and the reason a reader would click.
QUALITY CHECKS: Each link has a reader-benefit rationale. Anchors vary. Fewer
than six suggestions is an acceptable answer.
MISSING INFORMATION: If commercial importance was not supplied, rank by
topical relevance and say so.
HUMAN REVIEW: The editor approves before implementation.
Output format
A link table with anchors, destinations, scores, and reader-benefit reasons.
Quality checks
Reader benefit required; anchors vary; low-relevance links discarded.
If information is missing
Ranks by relevance alone if commercial importance is missing.
Human review
The editor approves before implementation.

PROMPT 13  Technical issue explanation
 TECHNICAL
Required inputs
The issue, the evidence, and the affected URLs.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are explaining a technical SEO issue to a non-specialist
stakeholder.
OBJECTIVE: Explain what is wrong, why it matters commercially, and what
should happen — without jargon and without exaggeration.
CONTEXT:
The issue: [DESCRIBE what you observed]
Evidence: [PASTE crawl data, log sample, or screenshots described]
Affected URLs: [count and examples]
Commercial importance of those pages: [DESCRIBE]
CONSTRAINTS: Do not estimate traffic loss or recovery. Do not claim certainty
about cause where the evidence is correlational. Explain in plain language a
non-technical stakeholder could repeat accurately.
PROCESS:
1. State what is happening, factually.
2. State what should be happening instead.
3. Explain the commercial consequence in terms of the affected pages.
4. State the confidence level in the diagnosis and what would raise it.
5. Recommend the next action — a fix, or a diagnostic if confidence is low.
OUTPUT FORMAT: A plain-language explanation under 200 words, then the
technical detail separately for engineering.
QUALITY CHECKS: No traffic estimates. Confidence stated explicitly. Plain
language genuinely plain.
MISSING INFORMATION: If evidence is thin, recommend a diagnostic rather than
a fix, and say what to gather.
HUMAN REVIEW: An engineer validates the diagnosis before a ticket is raised.
Output format
A plain-language explanation plus separate technical detail.
Quality checks
No traffic estimates; confidence stated; language genuinely plain.
If information is missing
Recommends a diagnostic rather than a fix when evidence is thin.
Human review
An engineer validates before ticketing.

PROMPT 16  Content refresh analysis
 CONTENT
Required inputs
The declining page, its performance history, and what now outranks it.
COPY-PASTE PROMPT
ROLE: You are diagnosing why a page has declined and what to do about it.
OBJECTIVE: Produce a specific refresh plan, or a recommendation to merge,
redirect, or remove.
CONTEXT:
The page: [PASTE full content]
Performance history: [PASTE traffic, rankings, and dates]
What now outranks it: [PASTE the current top results and what they cover]
Business importance: [why this page matters, or does not]
CONSTRAINTS: Distinguish a content problem from a technical problem from a
competitive problem — they need different responses. Do not recommend a
rewrite where the issue is intent mismatch or cannibalization.
PROCESS:
1. Identify when the decline started and what changed around then.
2. Compare our coverage with what now outranks us.
3. Check for intent drift — has the query's meaning changed?
4. Check for cannibalization against our own pages.
5. Recommend one action: update, merge, redirect, remove, or investigate.
OUTPUT FORMAT: The diagnosis with evidence, the single recommended action,
and — if updating — the specific changes in priority order.
QUALITY CHECKS: The diagnosis distinguishes problem types. One recommendation,
not a menu. Changes are specific rather than "improve the content".
MISSING INFORMATION: Without performance history, say the diagnosis is
speculative and recommend gathering it first.
HUMAN REVIEW: The content owner approves before republishing.
Output format
A diagnosis, one recommended action, and specific prioritized changes.
Quality checks
Problem types distinguished; one recommendation; changes specific.
If information is missing
Declares the diagnosis speculative without performance history.
Human review
The content owner approves before republishing.

SECTION 14 · SOPS
SEO templates and SOPs
Sixteen standard operating procedures. Each has an owner, a trigger, a quality gate, and an escalation
rule — because a procedure without those is a suggestion.
Build these as a single SOP database in Notion, one entry per procedure, with the fields below as properties. Filter by owner for a
personal view and by cadence for a calendar view. The templates these procedures reference live in the sections where they were
introduced — the one-page strategy in Section 01, the content brief in Section 04, the dev ticket in Section 06, the local landing
page in Section 09, and the brand fact sheet in Section 10.
SOP 01  Quarterly SEO strategy review
 SOP
Purpose
Keep the strategy connected to the business, and versioned.
Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
Two weeks before each quarter starts
Inputs
Last quarter’s reports, maturity assessment, performance data, business goals
STEPS
1
Re-run the maturity assessment and note the lowest dimension.
2
Review last quarter’s three bets against their success signals.
3
Reconstruct where effort actually went versus where the strategy said it should.
4
Revise the strategy one-pager, including the not-doing list.
5
Set three bets with a measurable signal and an owner each.
6
Version the document with a date and circulate it.
Output
A versioned one-page strategy with three owned bets
Quality gate
The gap between stated and actual effort is named explicitly
Escalation
If the lowest maturity dimension has not moved in two quarters, escalate to the business owner
Review cadence
Quarterly
SOP 02  Weekly performance review
 SOP
Purpose
Notice what changed while it is still cheap to respond to.
Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
Every Monday morning
Inputs
Search Console, analytics, rank tracking, technical alerts, AI visibility log
STEPS
1
Check technical alerts first — a crawl or index problem outranks everything else.
2
Review rankings and clicks against the previous four weeks, not the previous week.
3
Check conversions from organic against the quarter’s goal.
4
Scan the At Risk and Waiting For Review dashboard views.
5
Pick three priorities for the week and assign each one.
Output
Three assigned priorities in the Opportunities database
Quality gate
Every priority names an owner and a day it will happen
Escalation
Any sitewide indexing or traffic anomaly escalates immediately, outside this cadence
Review cadence
Weekly
TEMPLATES & SOPS
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai
Page 208 / 222

SOP 03  Keyword research and scoring
 SOP
Purpose
Maintain one prioritized keyword list everyone works from.
Owner
SEO specialist
Trigger
Quarterly, or when entering a new topic or market
Inputs
Demand databases, keyword tool exports, current rankings, business priorities
STEPS
1
Start from the Customer Problem and Question Bank databases, not a tool export.
2
Expand with tool data and label which keywords came from where.
3
Classify intent and stage, using SERP evidence where available.
4
Score against the ten criteria and apply the weights.
5
Map each keyword to exactly one owning page, or mark it a gap.
6
Run the cannibalization audit on anything already published.
Output
An updated Keyword Master Database with scores and mapping
Quality gate
No keyword maps to two pages; every score has its criteria recorded
Escalation
If more than a quarter of the list is unmappable, the problem is strategy, not keywords
Review cadence
Quarterly
SOP 04  Content brief and commission
 SOP
Purpose
Ensure nothing is written without a defined purpose and angle.
Owner
Content lead
Trigger
When a topic reaches the top of the build order
Inputs
Keyword, intent, cluster, our evidence, current top results, commercial destination
STEPS
1
Confirm the topic is not better served by updating an existing page.
2
Run the SERP analysis to establish what the query rewards.
3
Define the angle — what we say that current results do not.
4
Write the brief, including extractable passages and internal links.
5
Name the author and the reviewer, and set the due date.
Output
A complete brief in the Content database, status = brief
Quality gate
The angle is genuinely differentiated and the commercial link is specified
Escalation
If no differentiating angle exists, decline the commission and record why
Review cadence
Per piece
TEMPLATES & SOPS

SOP 05  Article QA before publication
 SOP
Purpose
Nothing goes live that would embarrass you if it were quoted.
Owner
Editor
Trigger
When a draft reaches review status
Inputs
The draft, the brief, the on-page checklist, the extractability checklist
STEPS
1
Check the draft answers the brief’s required coverage in order.
2
Run the on-page checklist and fix every failure.
3
Run the extractability checklist on each key passage in isolation.
4
Verify every claim has a source, or remove it.
5
Confirm internal links in and out, including the commercial destination.
6
Confirm the author is named with a relevant credential.
Output
A publish-or-return verdict with the specific fixes required
Quality gate
Unsourced claims are an automatic return, not a note for later
Escalation
Anything involving legal, medical, or financial claims goes to a qualified reviewer
Review cadence
Per piece
SOP 06  Monthly technical audit
 SOP
Purpose
Catch technical decay before it becomes a traffic event.
Owner
Technical SEO owner
Trigger
First week of each month, and after template releases
Inputs
Crawler, Search Console, log files, the previous audit
STEPS
1
Run a full crawl and compare against the previous month.
2
Check index coverage and investigate any change over five percent.
3
Review Core Web Vitals field data on key templates.
4
Check orphan pages, redirect chains, and broken links.
5
Score every new issue with the prioritization formula.
6
Create tickets for anything scoring above your threshold.
Output
Scored issues in the Technical Issues database, with tickets raised
Quality gate
Every issue has evidence attached and confidence above 3, or a diagnostic instead
Escalation
Critical severity issues bypass the cadence and go straight to engineering
Review cadence
Monthly

SOP 07  Technical issue to shipped fix
 SOP
Purpose
Make sure findings become deployed changes rather than a backlog.
Owner
Technical SEO owner with an engineering owner
Trigger
When an issue is ticketed
Inputs
The scored issue, its evidence, the affected URL list
STEPS
1
Write the ticket using the dev-ticket template.
2
Confirm acceptance criteria are testable by a non-SEO.
3
Agree the sprint and the engineering owner.
4
Verify on staging before deploy where possible.
5
Verify in production within 48 hours of deploy.
6
Record the verified date and close the issue.
Output
A verified fix with the date recorded
Quality gate
The issue is not closed until verified in production
Escalation
If a fix is deferred twice, escalate with the business impact restated
Review cadence
Per issue
SOP 08  Internal linking pass
 SOP
Purpose
Direct authority and readers towards commercially important pages.
Owner
SEO specialist
Trigger
Monthly, and after any batch of publishing
Inputs
Content database, internal link counts, commercial importance ratings
STEPS
1
Export every URL with its internal inbound link count.
2
Sort by commercial importance descending, link count ascending.
3
For the top twenty, identify relevant source pages.
4
Score each opportunity and discard anything below 3 on relevance.
5
Write descriptive anchors and implement in priority order.
6
Verify implementation and update the Internal Links database.
Output
Implemented links recorded with anchors and destinations
Quality gate
Every implemented link has a reader-benefit rationale recorded
Escalation
If a money page still has no internal links after two passes, raise it in the weekly review
Review cadence
Monthly

SOP 09  Content decay review
 SOP
Purpose
Recover declining pages before writing new ones.
Owner
Content lead
Trigger
Monthly
Inputs
Performance history by URL, current rankings, the Content database
STEPS
1
Identify every page whose traffic or rankings declined over 90 days.
2
For each, check what now outranks it and what changed.
3
Distinguish content, technical, intent, and cannibalization causes.
4
Decide: update, merge, redirect, remove, or investigate.
5
Add updates to the build order ahead of new commissions.
Output
A refresh queue with one decided action per declining page
Quality gate
Cause type is identified before an action is chosen
Escalation
A sitewide decline pattern is a technical or algorithmic question, not a content one
Review cadence
Monthly
SOP 10  Digital PR campaign
 SOP
Purpose
Earn coverage and citations from something you genuinely have.
Owner
Marketing or PR owner
Trigger
When a linkable asset is ready
Inputs
The asset, its methodology, permissions, target publications, spokesperson
STEPS
1
Confirm what may be published, with the data owner.
2
Develop angles and select the strongest supportable one.
3
Publish the asset with its methodology visible.
4
Build the target list by beat, not by domain metric.
5
Run outreach — three touches maximum per contact.
6
Log every outcome, including the refusals and the silence.
Output
Coverage, citations, and mentions logged in the Backlinks database
Quality gate
No claim in the pitch exceeds what the data supports
Escalation
Any request to pay for placement is declined and recorded
Review cadence
Per campaign

SOP 11  Backlink opportunity outreach
 SOP
Purpose
Pursue links without doing anything you would have to hide.
Owner
SEO or outreach owner
Trigger
When an asset and a target list exist
Inputs
The asset, qualified targets, the outreach templates
STEPS
1
Qualify each target on topical relevance first.
2
Confirm the asset genuinely improves their page.
3
Personalize each message with a specific reason.
4
Send, then follow up once, then stop.
5
Log the outcome regardless of what it was.
Output
Outcomes recorded against every contacted target
Quality gate
Nothing sent that would require concealment if published
Escalation
Decline any exchange, payment, or undisclosed arrangement, and record the decline
Review cadence
Per campaign

SOP 13  Monthly AI visibility run
 SOP
Purpose
Track AI visibility as a trend rather than an impression.
Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
First week of each month
Inputs
The fixed prompt universe, the brand fact sheet, the AI Visibility database
STEPS
1
Run every prompt with identical wording to previous months.
2
Record mention, citation, page cited, competitors named, and the verbatim passage.
3
Compare every statement about the brand against the fact sheet.
4
Log factual errors as the highest-priority findings.

Calculate the five scorecard measures and add to the trend.

SOP 14  Brand fact consistency correction
 SOP
Purpose
Keep the facts about your brand identical everywhere.
Owner
Marketing owner
Trigger
Quarterly, or whenever an inconsistency is found
Inputs
The canonical fact sheet and every public source describing the brand
STEPS
1
Audit every public source against the fact sheet.
2
Log each inconsistency with both versions quoted.
3
Decide the canonical version where the fact sheet is silent.
4
Correct owned properties first, then request third-party corrections.
5
Update the fact sheet and version it.
Output
A corrected source list and an updated, versioned fact sheet
Quality gate
One canonical wording per fact, with no third version introduced
Escalation
If a third party declines to correct a material error, record it and note it in the AI visibility log

SOP 15  Monthly SEO report
 SOP
Purpose
Report what changed and what is being decided, not what was done.
Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
First week of each month
Inputs
All metrics with sources and as-of dates, last month’s report, quarterly goals
STEPS
1
Assemble every figure with its source and as-of date.
2
Compare against last month and against the quarterly goal.
3
Separate what the data shows from what is being inferred.
4
State the three decisions the month requires.
5
Record what changes in the system as a result.
Output
A report under 500 words with three owned decisions
Quality gate
Every figure sourced and dated; nothing forecast
Escalation
A decision with no owner is not a decision — reassign before the report is filed
Review cadence
Monthly
SOP 16  Experiment design and evaluation
 SOP
Purpose
Make change attributable rather than coincidental.
Owner
SEO lead
Trigger
When the team disagrees about what would work
Inputs
The hypothesis, the baseline, the available volume
STEPS
1
Record the baseline with its source and date before changing anything.
2
Change exactly one variable and record what is held constant.
3
Set the minimum sample and the evaluation date in advance.
4
Write the decision rule before the experiment starts.
5
Evaluate on the date, apply the rule, record the outcome either way.
Output
A completed experiment record with an adopt, reject, or extend decision
Quality gate
One variable only; the decision rule predates the result
Escalation
If volume cannot support a conclusion, run structured interviews instead and say so
Review cadence
Per experiment

SECTION 15 · SCORECARDS
SEO scorecards
Nine scorecards using transparent criteria. Each is scored 0, 1, or 2 — coarse deliberately, because false
precision in SEO scoring is worse than none at all.
HOW TO READ EVERY SCORECARD IN THIS SECTION
These are diagnostic instruments, not measurements. They tell you where to look and make judgement consistent between people and
across quarters. They are not comparable to anyone else’s score, they do not predict performance, and the criteria are judgement
expressed as a scale. Score honestly — a flattering score is a wasted afternoon.
Website SEO health
Ten criteria covering the fundamentals. Run quarterly.
CRITERION
WHAT A 0 LOOKS LIKE
WHAT A 2 LOOKS LIKE
Indexation matches intent
Index count unknown or unexplained
Indexed pages match what we intend, and we can explain the difference
Crawl efficiency
No log file review has ever been done
Crawl budget goes to important pages, verified in logs
Speed on key templates
No field data collected
Field data collected and key templates within targets
Mobile experience
Never tested on a real device
Verified on real devices, no usability errors
Architecture depth
Money pages more than four clicks deep
Every important page within three clicks
Duplicate handling
Duplicates and parameters uncontrolled
Canonicals deliberate, parameters documented, facets controlled
Structured data
Absent or invalid
Present, valid, and matching visible content
Internal linking
Money pages under-linked
Commercial pages are among the best-linked pages
Content freshness
No refresh process exists
Every page reviewed at least twice yearly
Measurement
Cannot connect SEO to a business metric
At least one SEO metric maps to a business objective
SCORE
READ IT AS
NEXT ACTION
0–6
Foundational gaps
Fix these before anything else can compound
7–12
Functional, with real weaknesses
Pick the two lowest criteria this quarter
13–16
Solid
Maintain, and move to the next scorecard on your list
17–20
Strong
Protect it — review quarterly rather than rebuilding

The answer arrives after several paragraphs
The direct answer is in the first 100 words

Summarizes what already ranks

Key passages stand alone accurately

A hierarchy a reader could outline from
Conversion path
No next step, or four competing ones
One clear next step matched to the stage
Internal links

Descriptive anchors pointing at relevant commercial pages
Freshness
Undated, or visibly out of date
Published and updated dates visible and current

Ten criteria. Run after your first two monthly AI visibility passes, not before.

Entity consistency
Facts differ between our own sources
One canonical version of every fact, everywhere
Third-party corroboration
Only we describe us
Independent sources describe us consistently
Prompt universe

Present in the sources these systems actually use

The remaining six scorecards
Same 0–2 structure, same bands. Build them as one scorecard database in Notion with a type property.
SCORECARD
THE TEN CRITERIA, IN SHORT
Topic authority
Cluster completeness · pillar strength · supporting depth · interlinking within the cluster · coverage of the question
bank · original evidence · competitor comparison · rankings across the topic rather than one query · citations earned on
the topic · refresh currency
Technical health
Crawlability · indexability · render completeness · Core Web Vitals field data · mobile usability · redirect hygiene · status
code correctness · duplicate control · structured data validity · monitoring and alerting
Internal linking
Money page link counts · orphan pages · click depth · anchor descriptiveness · blog-to-commercial links · cluster
interlinking · link decay monitoring · navigation stability · breadcrumb presence · a tracked opportunity backlog
Authority
Linkable assets held · original data published · editorial coverage earned · unlinked mentions converted · directory and
profile completeness · expert visibility · community presence · review platform standing · citation authority · outreach
hygiene
Local visibility
Profile completeness · category accuracy · NAP consistency · citation coverage · review volume and recency · review
response rate · location page uniqueness · local schema · locally relevant content · multi-location consistency
Brand recommendation readiness
Findability across surfaces · entity clarity · fact consistency · third-party validation · review presence · comparison
content published · pricing transparency · extractable answers · category association · absence of unsupported claims
SCORE TWO, NOT NINE
Running all nine in one sitting produces a nine-page document nobody acts on. Run Website SEO Health first, because it gates everything
else. Then run the one covering whichever area your maturity assessment flagged as weakest. Return to the others next quarter.

SECTION 16 · ROADMAP
The 30-day implementation roadmap
Four weeks to a working system. This is deliberately smaller than you want it to be, because the version
you finish beats the version you abandon in week three.
WEEK 1  Audit, goals, baseline, workspace
 WEEK 1
By Friday you know where you stand and you have somewhere to put things.
DAY
WHAT HAPPENS
Day 1
Run the maturity assessment. Write down the lowest dimension.
Day 2
Full technical crawl. Record index coverage, orphan pages, and critical issues.
Day 3
Baseline everything — rankings, traffic, conversions, with dates. This is what you will compare against for the rest of the year.
Day 4
Build the Keywords and Content databases in Notion. Import what already exists.
Day 5
Write the one-page strategy, including the not-doing list. Version it.
QUALITY GATE
A maturity score, a dated baseline, two live databases, and a versioned strategy.
WEEK 2  Demand, keywords, content mapping, technical backlog
 WEEK 2
By Friday you know what to work on and in what order.
DAY
WHAT HAPPENS
Day 6
Mine customer language from calls, reviews, and tickets. Build the Question Bank.
Day 7
Keyword research from the demand evidence, not from a tool export.
Day 8
Score and prioritize the keyword list. Map each keyword to one owning page.
Day 9
Run the cannibalization audit and the content gap analysis.
Day 10
Score every technical issue and raise tickets for anything above threshold.
QUALITY GATE
A scored keyword list, a mapped content plan, and a prioritized technical backlog.
WEEK 3  Optimization, internal linking, authority, AI visibility
 WEEK 3
By Friday the first improvements have shipped and AI tracking has begun.
DAY
WHAT HAPPENS
Day 11
Internal linking pass. Start with money pages that have the fewest links.
Day 12
On-page and extractability pass on your five most commercially important pages.
Day 13
Build the brand fact sheet. Audit your own sources against it and correct what you own.
Day 14
Build the prompt universe. Cap it at 40 prompts and fix the wording.
Day 15
Run the first AI visibility pass. Record everything, including the errors.
QUALITY GATE
Links implemented, key pages optimized, a fact sheet, and the first AI visibility data point.
THE 30-DAY ROADMAP
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai
Page 219 / 222

WEEK 4  Reporting, learning, prioritization, next quarter
 WEEK 4
By Friday the system runs without you having to remember it.
DAY
WHAT HAPPENS
Day 16
Build the Reporting and Opportunities databases. Move everything loose into them.
Day 17
Write the first weekly report against the week-one baseline.
Day 18
Set up the SOP database. Add the six SOPs you will actually run.
Day 19
Schedule the weekly rhythm in a calendar, with owners attached.
Day 20
Write the next-quarter roadmap from what the month revealed.
QUALITY GATE
A live reporting loop, six running SOPs, a scheduled rhythm, and a next-quarter plan.
IF YOU HAVE LESS THAN A DAY A WEEK
Do week one in full — it takes about six hours and it is the only part where skipping steps invalidates everything after it. Then run week
two across a fortnight and stop. A baseline, a scored keyword list, and a mapped content plan is already a real system. Weeks three and
four can wait for the following quarter.
THE 30-DAY ROADMAP

SECTION 17 · VERIFICATION
Final readiness checklist
Twelve areas. If you can tick most of these, you have an SEO operating system rather than a list of
tactics.
Strategy
 A one-page strategy exists, versioned, with a not-doing list
Three bets have measurable signals and named owners
The maturity assessment has been run and the weakest dimension
named
Keywords
 One keyword list exists that everyone works from
Every keyword is scored with its criteria recorded
Each keyword maps to exactly one owning page
Content
 Every page has a stated purpose and a commercial destination
A brief standard exists and is used before commissioning
A decay review runs monthly and produces refresh decisions
Technical SEO
 A scored technical backlog exists with engineering owners
A crawl runs monthly and after template releases
Critical issues have a defined escalation route
Internal linking
 Money pages are among the best-linked pages on the site
Orphan pages are identified and resolved
A link opportunity backlog is tracked and worked
Authority
 At least one linkable asset exists that others would reference
Outreach is logged, including refusals and silence
Nothing in the programme would need to be hidden
Local SEO
 A canonical NAP record exists per location
Listings are audited monthly against it
Reviews are responded to within a defined window
AI visibility
 A fixed prompt universe exists and is re-run monthly
A brand fact sheet exists and public sources match it
Factual errors trigger correction at source
Measurement
 A baseline exists, with dates, for everything you track
Every reported figure has a source and an as-of date
At least one SEO metric maps to a business objective
Governance
 Every database has an owner and a review cadence
The weekly rhythm is in a calendar, not in someone’s memory
Decisions are recorded together with their reasoning
Human review
 Nothing publishes without a named reviewer
AI-drafted output is reviewed before it goes anywhere
Claims are sourced or removed, never softened
Data quality
 Demand evidence is verbatim, not paraphrased
Unknown values are recorded as unknown rather than estimated
Where a tool supplies a metric, the tool is named
FINAL CHECKLIST
The AI SEO Brain  ·  Offered by Klaym AI — klaym.ai
Page 221 / 222

FINAL WORD
Build your SEO brain.
Modern SEO is no longer one channel, one ranking report, or one content calendar. It is a connected
system that helps your brand become easier to find, understand, trust, cite, and recommend.
Everything in this playbook is designed to live in one workspace and run on a weekly rhythm. Not because systems are inherently
virtuous, but because the alternative — scattered tactics, decisions reopened every month, and a strategy that exists only in one
person’s head — is what makes SEO feel unpredictable when it is mostly just undocumented.
Start with the parts that compound. The baseline, so you can tell whether anything is working. The keyword list, so the same
decisions stop being reopened. The internal linking pass, because it is free and fast. The brand fact sheet, because entity consistency
is the cheapest AI visibility work available to anyone. Then let the weekly rhythm carry the rest.

DAYS TO RUNNING
The same shift is happening in how buyers discover brands.
An increasing share of evaluation now happens before anyone visits a website. A buyer asks an assistant who the credible options
are, and a shortlist forms without your involvement. Being in that answer is not a different discipline from SEO — it is the same
discipline, measured on a new surface.
Klaym AI helps B2B brands track, analyze, and improve their visibility across AI search and answer engines including ChatGPT,
Claude, Gemini, Perplexity, and Google AI Overviews — where you appear, why competitors get recommended instead, and what
would change that.

reviewable, not a measurement of objective truth. All names, figures, and examples shown in brackets are placeholders. Every output produced with the prompts in
